- `zone = (name: str)`: start timing a part of the frame, until zone_end() (zones nest; their times show in the overlay; a zone entered several times in a frame shows their total); costs a check when the statistics are off
- `zone_end = ()`
- `zone = (name: str, f: () -> void)`: f, timed as a zone

## Controls and gamepads

```gdscript
# example: controls and gamepads
window("Controls", 960, 540)

# actions with their default keys and buttons; the player can change them (ui_controls)
action("jump", [.key(.space), .key(.w), .button(.a)])
action("left", [.key(.a), .key(.left), .stick(0, -1), .button(.left)])
action("right", [.key(.d), .key(.right), .stick(0, 1), .button(.right)])

x = 480.0
y = 0.0
vy = 0.0
settings = false

update = (dt: f64):
    global settings, vy, y
    if key_pressed(.tab): settings = not settings
    if settings: return
    x += action_axis("left", "right") * 300 * dt        # -1 to 1 (a stick gives in between)
    if action_pressed("jump") and y == 0.0: vy = 500.0
    vy -= 1200 * dt
    y = max(0.0, y + vy * dt)

draw = ():
    global settings
    clear(rgb(0.1, 0.1, 0.14))
    rect(0, 440, 960, 100, rgb(0.25, 0.3, 0.25))
    rect(x - 20, 400 - y, 40, 40, ORANGE)
    text("move: A D or arrows   jump: space or W   Tab: change the controls   ({gamepads().len()} gamepads)", vec2(20, 20), 20)
    if settings:
        ui_begin("controls", vec2(480, 100))
        ui_controls()                    # choose an action, then press its new key or button
        if ui_button("Back") or ui_back(): settings = false
        ui_end()
```

### controls.jo

Controls the player can change. The game names its actions and gives each its default inputs, then asks for actions, never for keys; ui_controls() is a settings screen where the player rebinds them (choose one, press the new key or button), and the bindings are saved.

```gdscript
action("jump", [.key(.space), .button(.a)])
action("left", [.key(.a), .key(.left), .stick(0, -1), .button(.left)])
action("right", [.key(.d), .key(.right), .stick(0, 1), .button(.right)])
...
if action_pressed("jump"): jump()
x = action_axis("left", "right")            # -1..1, analog on a stick

# in the settings menu:
ui_begin("settings", vec2(640, 140))
ui_controls()
if ui_button("Back") or ui_back(): ...
ui_end()
```

Local multiplayer: a player number reads that gamepad (keys and the mouse count for player 0); give players on one keyboard actions of their own ("p2 jump").

- `enum Input: key(k: Key), button(b: PadButton), mouse(m: Mouse), stick(axis: int, dir: int)`: an input: a key, a gamepad button, a mouse button, or a stick or trigger pushed one way (axis: 0 left x, 1 left y, 2 right x, 3 right y, 4 left trigger, 5 right trigger; dir: 1 or -1)
- `action = (name: str, defaults: Input[])`: declare an action and its default inputs (once, before reading it: again, nothing changes); a binding the player saved replaces the defaults
- `input_value = (x: Input, player: int = -1) -> f64`: how far input x is pushed, 0 to 1, for player (-1: anyone)
- `action_value = (name: str, player: int = -1) -> f64`: how far the action is pushed, 0 to 1 (the most of its inputs)
- `action_down = (name: str, player: int = -1) -> bool`
- `action_pressed = (name: str, player: int = -1) -> bool`: true in the frame the action was pressed (a stick: when it goes past halfway)
- `action_released = (name: str, player: int = -1) -> bool`
- `action_axis = (neg: str, pos: str, player: int = -1) -> f64`: from -1 (neg) to 1 (pos): action_axis("left", "right")
- `action_vec = (left: str, right: str, up: str, down: str, player: int = -1) -> vec2`: a direction (y down), at most 1 long

#### Bindings

- `bindings = (name: str) -> Input[]`
- `set_bindings = (name: str, inputs: Input[])`
- `reset_controls = ()`: every action back to its defaults
- `input_name = (x: Input) -> str`: what an input is called, for showing it ("Space", "Pad A", "Left stick right")

#### Rebinding

- `input_pressed = () -> Input?`: the input the player pressed first this frame (a key, a button, a click, a stick pushed most of the way), if any
- `rebinding = () -> bool`: whether ui_controls is waiting for a new input (the game's own keys should wait too), or took one this frame
- `ui_controls = (names: str[] = [], labels: str[] = [], rows: int = 0)`: a row per action (its name, and its inputs), in a menu (between ui_begin and ui_end), and a row to put the defaults back. Choosing an action waits for its new input: a key replaces its first key (or the mouse), a gamepad's button or stick its first gamepad input; Escape cancels. names: the actions shown (and what they are called), in order: all of them if none given. rows: how many are shown at once (0: as many as fit on the screen, with room for two items after); the list scrolls to the chosen one, and with the mouse wheel.

### gamepad.jo

Gamepads and joysticks: up to eight at once, each by its number, with a standard layout (Xbox-style names), and every device's own buttons and axes as they are (flight sticks, wheels).

```gdscript
if gamepad_pressed(.a): jump()            # any gamepad
move = left_stick()                        # vec2 in -1..1, y down, with a dead zone
loop pad in gamepads():                    # (local multiplayer: one player per pad)
    players[pad].move = left_stick(pad)
```

A controller keeps its number while it is plugged in (players stay who they are); one plugged in later takes a free number. input_axis() also follows the sticks and d-pads, so keyboard games work with a pad. Linux builds read the joystick devices (/dev/input/js0..7), Windows builds XInput (four controllers: what Windows maps to the Xbox layout), web builds the browser's Gamepad API, macOS builds Apple's GameController framework.

- `enum PadButton: a, b, x, y, lb, rb, back, start, home, lstick, rstick, up, down, left, right`
- `const MAX_GAMEPADS = 8`
- `gamepads = () -> int[]`: the numbers of the gamepads connected
- `gamepad_connected = () -> bool`
- `gamepad_connected = (pad: int) -> bool`
- `gamepad_name = (pad: int) -> str`: what the device calls itself ("" if it does not say)
- `gamepad_down = (pad: int, b: PadButton) -> bool`: a button of pad, or (without a pad) of any gamepad
- `gamepad_pressed = (pad: int, b: PadButton) -> bool`
- `gamepad_released = (pad: int, b: PadButton) -> bool`
- `gamepad_down = (b: PadButton) -> bool`
- `gamepad_pressed = (b: PadButton) -> bool`
- `gamepad_released = (b: PadButton) -> bool`
- `gamepad_axis = (pad: int, i: int) -> f64`: axis value in -1..1 (triggers 0..1): 0 left x, 1 left y, 2 right x, 3 right y, 4 left trigger, 5 right trigger; without a pad, the gamepad that has it furthest from rest
- `gamepad_axis = (i: int) -> f64`
- `left_stick = (pad: int) -> vec2`
- `right_stick = (pad: int) -> vec2`
- `left_stick = () -> vec2`: (without a pad: the one pushed furthest)
- `right_stick = () -> vec2`
- `joystick_buttons = (pad: int) -> int`: any device's own buttons and axes, as it numbers them (a flight stick's hat and throttle, a wheel's pedals): how many it has, and their state
- `joystick_axes = (pad: int) -> int`
- `joystick_button = (pad: int, i: int) -> bool`
- `joystick_pressed = (pad: int, i: int) -> bool`
- `joystick_axis = (pad: int, i: int) -> f64`

## Drawing in 2D

```gdscript
# example: drawing in 2D
window("Shapes", 960, 540)

t = 0.0
update = (dt: f64): t += dt

draw = ():
    clear(rgb(0.1, 0.1, 0.14))
    rect(40, 40, 200, 120, rgb(0.9, 0.3, 0.3))            # x, y (top left), width, height
    rect_outline(270, 40, 200, 120, 4, WHITE)
    circle(vec2(600, 100), 60, hsv(t * 0.1, 0.7, 1.0))    # a color that changes
    ring(vec2(800, 100), 60, 8, YELLOW)
    arc(vec2(800, 100), 40, 8, 0.0, t % TAU, CYAN)
    line(vec2(40, 220), vec2(460, 300), 6, GREEN)
    polygon([vec2(560, 220), vec2(680, 260), vec2(620, 340)], PURPLE)
    circle_gradient(vec2(820, 300), 80, rgba(1, 0.8, 0.3, 1), rgba(1, 0.4, 0.1, 0))

    # transforms: what follows is moved, turned and scaled
    push_transform()
    translate(240, 430)
    rotate(t)
    rect(-50, -50, 100, 100, BLUE)
    pop_transform()
    text("text at any size", vec2(420, 420), 40, WHITE)
```

### draw2d.jo

2D drawing: batched shapes, textured sprites, SDF text, transforms, blend modes and render targets. Coordinates are in the screen's units (1280 x 720 unless the program says otherwise, whatever the window's real size), with (0, 0) at the top-left and y down; on a render target, its pixels.

- `struct Texture`
  ```gdscript
  struct Texture:
      id: int
      width: int
      height: int
  ```
- `struct RenderTarget`
  ```gdscript
  struct RenderTarget:
      texture: Texture
      fbo: int
      depth: int
  ```
- `enum Blend: alpha, add, multiply, premultiplied`
- `clear = (c: vec4 = vec4(0, 0, 0, 1))`
- `blend = (b: Blend)`
- `clip = (pos: vec2, size: vec2)`: 2D drawing after this shows only inside the rectangle, until no_clip(), the next frame or another target (draw_to); for a scrolling list, a panel, a minimap. The rectangle is in the units of the 2D transform, as what is drawn is (under translate, scale or camera2d_fit it is where a rect() of it shows; turned, the box around that); the clip is then kept where it is on the screen, whatever transform comes after
- `clip = (r: Rect)`: the same, with a Rect: clip(r) around a scrolling list's box
- `no_clip = ()`: drawing shows everywhere again (and a hud_scroll list's bar, drawn over its rows, is drawn now, as they are done)
- `push_transform = ()`
- `pop_transform = ()`
- `translate = (x: f64, y: f64)`
- `rotate = (angle: f64)`
- `scale = (sx: f64, sy: f64)`
- `reset_transform = ()`
- `camera2d = (target: vec2, zoom: f64 = 1.0, angle: f64 = 0.0)`: 2D camera: `target` appears at the screen center, scaled by zoom
- `camera2d_fit = (world: Rect, on: Rect)`: a 2D camera showing the area `world` (in the world's units) as large as it fits inside `on` (a part of the screen: visible_rect() for all of it), centered there, its shape kept: a board above the buttons on a phone held upright,
  ```gdscript
  v = visible_rect()
  camera2d_fit(Rect(0, 0, COLS * 64, ROWS * 64), Rect(v.x, v.y, v.w, v.h - 200))
  ```
- `screen_to_world = (p: vec2) -> vec2`: convert a screen position to world coordinates under the current transform; in update (before draw sets one), under the last camera2d: screen_to_world(mouse_pos()) is where the mouse points
- `world_to_screen = (p: vec2) -> vec2`: where a world position appears on the screen under the current transform (the other way; in update, the last camera2d's)

#### Effects: a shader of your own on 2D drawing

- `struct Pixel2D`: An effect is a fragment function run for each pixel of what is drawn while it is on (sprites, shapes, text): it gets the pixel (where it is, its place in the texture, the color it is drawn with) and the uniforms, Effect2D[P] holding your parameters P; base_color gives what drawing would have made of the pixel without it.
  ```gdscript
  struct Glow: strength: f32
  glow_fs = (p: Pixel2D, u: Effect2D[Glow]) -> vec4:
      c = base_color(p, u)
      vec4(c.rgb * u.params.strength, c.a)
  glow = make_shader(shader(effect_vs, glow_fs))      # once
  ...
  use_effect(glow, Glow(1.5))                         # in draw: what is drawn now glows
  sprite(ship, pos)
  no_effect()
  ```
  ```gdscript
  struct Pixel2D:
      pos: vec4               # where on the screen, in pixels from the bottom left (z, w: 0 and 1)
      uv: vec2                # where in the texture (0 to 1)
      color: vec4             # the color it is drawn with
      mode: f32               # (1 for text: its texture holds distances to the letters' edges)
  ```
- `struct Effect2D[P]`
  ```gdscript
  struct Effect2D[P]:
      proj: mat4
      texture: Texture        # what is being drawn (white for shapes)
      params: P
  ```
- `effect_vs[P] = (v: __Vert2D, u: Effect2D[P]) -> Pixel2D`: the vertex function of every effect: shader(effect_vs, your_fragment_function)
- `base_color[P] = (p: Pixel2D, u: Effect2D[P]) -> vec4`: the pixel's color as 2D drawing makes it without an effect
- `use_effect[P] = (sh: Shader, params: P)`: 2D drawing from here on goes through effect sh (made with shader(effect_vs, ...)), with params, until no_effect() or the end of the frame
- `no_effect = ()`: back to drawing without an effect

#### Textures

- `texture = (img: Image, smooth: bool = smooth_textures) -> Texture`: upload an image to the GPU (smooth: see smooth_textures)
- `set_repeat = (t: Texture, on: bool)`: a texture that repeats past its edges, for sprite_region (and a shader's sample) beyond them: a source rectangle wider than the texture shows it again and again. Off (the default), what is past an edge is that edge's pixels, stretched. A whole texture repeats, not a part of a sheet: sprite_tiled repeats any part, on every texture.
- `update_texture = (t: Texture, img: Image)`
- `load_texture = (file: u8[], smooth: bool = smooth_textures) -> Texture`: a texture from a PNG or JPEG file's bytes: `load_texture(embed("hero.png"))`; smooth: false for sharp pixels when it is drawn larger (pixel art), as every texture is after pixel_art()
- `free_texture = (t: Texture)`: give back a texture's memory (it may not be drawn after): for textures made while the game runs, a save's thumbnail, a level's tiles

#### Render targets

- `render_target = (w: int, h: int, smooth: bool = true) -> RenderTarget`
- `render_image = (w: int, h: int, draw: () -> void) -> Image`: a picture of w x h pixels drawn by draw (with what `draw = ():` uses, in pixels from its top left corner), at any time: a chart a script saves, a texture made at the start. Before any window is open it needs none: the software renderer draws it.
  ```gdscript
  img = render_image(600, 400, ():
      clear(WHITE)
      line(vec2(40, 360), vec2(560, 40), 2, BLUE))
  save_png(img, "chart.png")
  ```
- `free_render_target = (rt: RenderTarget)`: give back a render target's memory (it may not be used after)
- `draw_to = (rt: RenderTarget)`: draw into a render target until draw_to_screen() is called
- `draw_to_screen = ()`

#### Batching

- `flush = ()`

#### Shapes

- `rect = (x: f64, y: f64, w: f64, h: f64, c: vec4)`
- `rect = (pos: vec2, size: vec2, c: vec4)`
- `rect = (r: Rect, c: vec4)`
- `rect_gradient = (x: f64, y: f64, w: f64, h: f64, top: vec4, bottom: vec4)`: rectangle with a vertical color gradient
- `rect_outline = (x: f64, y: f64, w: f64, h: f64, thickness: f64, c: vec4)`
- `rect_outline = (r: Rect, thickness: f64, c: vec4)`
- `triangle = (a: vec2, b: vec2, c: vec2, col: vec4)`
- `triangle_gradient = (a: vec2, b: vec2, c: vec2, ca: vec4, cb: vec4, cc: vec4)`: triangle with a color per corner (colors blend across it)
- `quad_gradient = (a: vec2, b: vec2, c: vec2, d: vec2, ca: vec4, cb: vec4, cc: vec4, cd: vec4)`: quad (a, b, c, d in order around it) with a color per corner
- `circle = (center: vec2, radius: f64, c: vec4, segments: int = 0)`
- `ellipse = (center: vec2, radii: vec2, c: vec4, segments: int = 0)`: an ellipse: radii.x across, radii.y up and down (a shadow under a card or a character)
- `rect_rounded = (x: f64, y: f64, w: f64, h: f64, radius: f64, c: vec4)`: a rectangle with rounded corners, each a quarter circle of this radius (a card, a button)
- `rect_rounded_outline = (x: f64, y: f64, w: f64, h: f64, radius: f64, thickness: f64, c: vec4)`: its outline, thickness wide inside it
- `circle_gradient = (center: vec2, radius: f64, inner: vec4, outer: vec4, segments: int = 48)`: circle that fades from `inner` color at the center to `outer` at the edge (glows, soft lights)
- `ring = (center: vec2, radius: f64, thickness: f64, c: vec4, segments: int = 48)`
- `arc = (center: vec2, radius: f64, thickness: f64, start: f64, stop: f64, c: vec4, segments: int = 0)`: part of a ring: from angle `start` to `stop` (radians, clockwise on screen)
- `line = (a: vec2, b: vec2, thickness: f64, c: vec4)`
- `polyline = (points: vec2[], thickness: f64, c: vec4, closed: bool = false)`: lines through the points, one band without gaps at the bends (mitred: a sharp bend's point cut off at three times the thickness)
- `polygon = (points: vec2[], c: vec4)`: a filled polygon, its points in order around it (either way round); a concave one (a star, an L) is cut into triangles

#### Sprites

- `sprite = (t: Texture, pos: vec2, size: vec2 = vec2(0, 0), tint: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), angle: f64 = 0.0, origin: vec2 = vec2(0, 0))`: (where each puts a picture, and origin: the table in sprites.jo) draw a texture with its top left corner at `pos` (origin: another point of it), optionally scaled (size: in the screen's units; vec2(0, 0): its own), turned and tinted
- `sprite_region = (t: Texture, src: vec4, center: vec2, size: vec2, tint: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), angle: f64 = 0.0, origin: vec2 = vec2(0.5, 0.5))`: draw part of a texture (src = x, y, w, h in its pixels) with its middle at `center` (origin: another point of it), size big. A src past the texture's edges shows the edge pixels stretched, unless set_repeat(t, true) (the texture again); sprite_tiled repeats a part of one.
- `sprite_tiled = (t: Texture, area: Rect, offset: vec2 = vec2(0, 0), scale: f64 = 1.0, tint: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), src: vec4 = vec4(0, 0, 0, 0))`: fill `area` with copies of a texture side by side (or of a part of it, src: x, y, w, h in its pixels), each `scale` times its size, the copies shifted by `offset`; those at area's edges are cut. A background that scrolls and repeats (Godot's motion_mirroring), drawn without a camera, here a third as fast as the camera: A strip repeats one way when area is as tall (or wide) as a copy. Any texture, any part of one: no set_repeat needed.
  ```gdscript
  sprite_tiled(hills, Rect(0, 90, 480, 180), vec2(-cam.x / 3.0, 0))
  ```
- `draw_target = (rt: RenderTarget, pos: vec2 = vec2(0, 0), size: vec2 = vec2(0, 0), tint: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1))`: draw a render target's texture (flipped: render targets are stored bottom-up)

#### Text

- `set_font = (f: Font)`: the font text() and text_width() use (load_font makes one); set_font() goes back to the built-in
- `set_font = ()`
- `text_width = (s: str, size: f64 = 20.0, mono: bool = false) -> f64`: how wide text is drawn (the widest of its lines)
- `fit_text = (s: str, width: f64, size: f64 = 20.0) -> str`: s as it fits in a line `width` wide: cut short, ending in …, when it is wider (a long name on a button, a table's cell)
- `text_height = (s: str, size: f64 = 20.0) -> f64`: how tall text is drawn: its lines, each size * 1.25 below the one before
- `enum Align:`
  ```gdscript
  enum Align:
      left
      center
      right
  ```
- `wrap_text = (s: str, width: f64, size: f64 = 20.0) -> str[]`: s broken into lines no wider than width, at spaces, and between the characters of Chinese, Japanese and Korean (a word wider than that has a line of its own; a line break in s starts a new line; 。、」 and the like stay at the end of a line, not the start of the next)
- `text_wrapped = (s: str, pos: vec2, width: f64, size: f64 = 20.0, color: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), align: Align = .left, shadow: vec4 = vec4(0, 0, 0, 0), chars: int = -1) -> f64`: text in a column `width` wide from pos (its top-left corner), broken into lines at spaces, each line to the left, centered or to the right; the height it took (LÖVE's printf). chars: only the first that many characters, laid out where the whole text puts them (dialogue that types itself out does not jump to the next line midway through a word)
- `text = (s: str, pos: vec2, size: f64 = 20.0, color: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), shadow: vec4 = vec4(0, 0, 0, 0), mono: bool = false, chars: int = -1)`: draw text with its top-left corner at `pos`. The built-in font has ASCII, the accented letters of European languages (é ñ ü ł ő č ş ș), symbols (° ² ½ ± × ÷), Cyrillic (Привет), Greek (θ ω π Δ λ μ), − √ ≈ ≠ ≤ ≥ ∞ ∑ ∫ ∂ ∝ ⋅ ∇ ∈, arrows (→ ← ↑ ↓ ↔), sub- and superscript digits (v₀, x⁴, 10⁻⁹), ′ ″ and ✓ ✗; characters a font does not have show as ? (load_font: others); shadow: a color drawn under it, a little down and right, to read over a busy scene (`shadow = rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.7)`). mono: each character in a cell of the same width, so columns made with "{name:<12} {score:>5}" line up (a scoreboard, a table, a roguelike's map). chars: only the first that many characters (dialogue appearing letter by letter: where the rest will be does not move); -1: all
- `text_rich = (s: str, pos: vec2, size: f64 = 20.0, color: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), width: f64 = 0.0, chars: int = -1, shadow: vec4 = vec4(0, 0, 0, 0)) -> f64`: text with parts of it styled where they are: [color=#ff8040]...[/color] (or a name: red, green, blue, yellow, orange, purple, cyan, magenta, gray, white, black), [b]...[/b] bold, [i]...[/i] slanted, [[ for a [. width above 0: lines broken to fit it (as wrap_text). chars: only the first that many characters (dialogue typing itself out). The height it took.
  ```gdscript
  text_rich("You found [color=yellow]the brass key[/color]. It is [i]warm[/i].", vec2(40, 600), 24, WHITE, width = 800)
  ```
- `text_centered = (s: str, center: vec2, size: f64 = 20.0, color: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), shadow: vec4 = vec4(0, 0, 0, 0), mono: bool = false)`: text centered on `center` (horizontally and vertically; each of its lines centered). The built-in font is proportional ("i" is narrower than "@"): a grid of characters, a roguelike's map, draws each one centered in its cell

### font.jo

Fonts: load_font reads a TrueType or OpenType font (.ttf, .otf, or the first font of a .ttc) and makes the characters text() draws with, sharp at any size (signed distance fields). set_font chooses the font text() and text_width() use; set_font() goes back to the built-in one.

```gdscript
title_font = load_font(embed("PressStart2P.ttf"))
draw = ():
    set_font(title_font)
    text_centered("GAME OVER", vec2(640, 300), 64)
    set_font()
```

It has the printable ASCII characters, Latin-1 and Latin Extended-A (accented letters), common punctuation (quotes, dashes, the euro sign), and any others listed in `extra`. A character the font does not have is drawn with the built-in one (Latin in a Japanese font), else as ?.

Japanese, Chinese or Korean text needs a font with them (Noto Sans CJK, which most systems have), and its characters in `extra`: give it all the text that will be shown (load_font(file, extra = story)). wrap_text breaks lines between their characters. (Variable fonts with CFF2 outlines are not read: a static .otf or .ttf of the same font is.)

- `struct Font`
  ```gdscript
  struct Font:
      texture: Texture
      slots: {int: int}           # a character (its code point) -> its cell in the atlas
      ascii: int[]                # (the same for the first 128, faster; -1: none)
      adv: f64[]                  # each cell's advance, in pixels at `px`
      cols: int                   # cells in a row of the atlas
      cell: f64                   # a cell's size, in atlas pixels
      px: f64                     # the size the characters were made at
      base: f64                   # the baseline's height in a cell
      spread: f64                 # distance (in atlas pixels) the field fades over
  ```

#### Making the atlas

- `load_font = (file: u8[], extra: str = "") -> Font`: a font from a TrueType file (it stops the program with a message if it cannot read it)

### screen.jo

How a game's picture fits windows of any size and shape.

A game is drawn in 2D units: the size given to window() (its design size; design_size changes it while the game runs), so the same coordinates work in any window. Two settings decide how that maps to the window's pixels:

```gdscript
screen_fit(mode): what a window of another shape shows
    .expand     the design area, scaled to fit, and more around it (the default: no bars,
                nothing cut; visible_rect() says what is shown)
    .letterbox  just the design area, scaled to fit, with bars (letterbox_color) around it
    .crop       the design area scaled to fill the window: what does not fit is cut off
    .stretch    the design area stretched to the window (shapes distort)
    .native     no scaling: one 2D unit is one pixel as the desktop counts them (on a screen
                at 200%, or a phone's, two of the screen's own; screen_size() is the
                window's size in them): a desktop tool's layout

render_resolution(w, h): draw at a fixed resolution, scaled up to the window, by default
    with sharp pixels (nearest) and in whole steps (2x, 3x...); 2D units stay the design size
    (give both the same shape). The picture letterboxes (or crops/stretches with those
    fits). render_resolution(0, 0) goes back to the window's resolution.
pixel_art(): render_resolution at the design size: for a game designed at 320 x 180, one
    2D unit is one sharp pixel. (A window for a design that small opens a whole number of
    times larger.) Textures loaded after it are sharp too (smooth_textures).
render_scale(s): draw at a fraction of the window's pixels (0.5: a quarter of them), scaled
    up smoothly: faster where pixels cost (3D, the software renderer).
full_resolution(): in draw, the rest of the frame at the window's own resolution, in the same
    2D units: a HUD over pixel art whose text stays sharp.
```

Drawing that needs it goes to an offscreen canvas, put on the window when the frame ends; the mouse is mapped back the same way (mouse_pos() is in 2D units).

- `enum Fit: expand, letterbox, crop, stretch, native`
- `screen_fit = (mode: Fit)`
- `letterbox_color = (c: vec4)`
- `render_resolution = (w: int, h: int, smooth: bool = false, whole: bool = true)`
- `pixel_art = (smooth: bool = false)`: (and textures made after it are sharp: smooth_textures)
- `render_scale = (s: f64)`
- `full_resolution = ()`: the rest of this frame's drawing at the window's own resolution, over what was drawn at the low one of pixel_art() (or render_resolution, render_scale), in the same 2D units: a HUD whose text stays sharp, where text a few pixels tall at 320 x 180 is unreadable. Without a low resolution it does nothing.
  ```gdscript
  draw = ():
      draw_tilemap(level)          # pixel art
      full_resolution()
      text("Score {score}", vec2(4, 4), 8)
  ```

### softgl.jo

- `soft_rendering = () -> bool`

## Images, sprites and tilemaps

```gdscript
# example: images, textures and sprites
window("Sprites", 960, 540)

# an image made pixel by pixel (load_image(embed("hero.png")) reads one from a file)
img = image(16, 16)
loop y in 0..16:
    loop x in 0..16:
        d = length(vec2(x - 7.5, y - 7.5))
        if d < 7.0: img.set(x, y, hsv(y / 40.0, 0.8, 1.0))
        if d < 3.0: img.set(x, y, WHITE)
ball = texture(img, false)               # on the GPU; false: sharp pixels when scaled

t = 0.0
update = (dt: f64): t += dt

draw = ():
    clear(rgb(0.12, 0.12, 0.16))
    sprite(ball, vec2(100, 100))                                   # its own size
    sprite(ball, vec2(200, 100), vec2(128, 128))                   # scaled up
    sprite(ball, vec2(400, 100), vec2(128, 128), rgb(0.4, 1, 0.6), t)   # tinted and turned
    loop i in 0..12:
        a = i / 12.0 * TAU + t
        sprite(ball, vec2(700 + 120 * cos(a), 300 + 120 * sin(a)), vec2(32, 32))
```

### image.jo

Images: RGBA8 pixel buffers, PNG decoding (with a built-in inflate; JPEG: jpeg.jo), procedural helpers.

- `struct Image`
  ```gdscript
  struct Image:
      width: int
      height: int
      pixels: u8[]          # RGBA, row-major, top row first
  ```
- `image = (w: int, h: int, fill: vec4 = vec4(0, 0, 0, 0)) -> Image`
- `set = (img: mut Image, x: int, y: int, c: vec4)`
- `get = (img: Image, x: int, y: int) -> vec4`
- `resized = (img: Image, w: int, h: int) -> Image`: the image at another size (a save's thumbnail, an icon): each new pixel the average of the pixels it covers when smaller, smoothly between them when larger

#### Inflate (RFC 1951)

- `struct Inflate`
  ```gdscript
  struct Inflate:
      src: u8[]
      pos: int
      bitbuf: int
      bitcnt: int
      out: u8[]
      err: bool
  ```
- `struct Huff`
  ```gdscript
  struct Huff:
      count: int[]      # codes of each length (0..15)
      symbol: int[]     # symbols ordered by code
  ```
- `inflate = (src: u8[], start: int = 0) -> u8[]?`

#### PNG

- `load_png = (file: u8[]) -> Image?`: decode a PNG file; none if the data is not a supported PNG
- `load_image = (file: u8[]) -> Image`: load an image from an embedded file: load_image(embed("hero.png"))

#### Deflate (RFC 1951)

- `deflate = (src: u8[]) -> u8[]`: compress bytes into a raw deflate stream (inflate reads it back)

#### PNG writing

- `encode_png = (img: Image) -> u8[]`
- `save_png = (img: Image, path: str) -> bool`
- `screenshot = () -> Image`: read what has been drawn into an image: the window's pixels (on a scaled display more than the size asked for in window(): resized(img, w, h) makes it smaller), or the render target being drawn to (draw_to)

### jpeg.jo

JPEG images: load_jpeg (and load_image, which tells PNG and JPEG apart). Baseline and progressive JPEGs (Huffman coded, 8-bit), any sampling (4:4:4, 4:2:2, 4:2:0...), grayscale, RGB and Adobe's CMYK, restart markers. As libjpeg decodes them by default: its integer inverse DCT, smooth upsampling of halved color, its color conversion. Not read: arithmetic coding, lossless and 12-bit JPEGs (rare): none, as for a damaged file.

- `load_jpeg = (file: u8[]) -> Image?`: decode a JPEG file into RGBA pixels; none if it is not one (or one of the kinds not read)

### sprites.jo

Sprite sheets and animations. A sheet is a texture cut into frames: a grid of equal frames (sprite_sheet), or the frames and animations Aseprite exports (load_aseprite: File > Export Sprite Sheet with "JSON Data" on; each tag becomes an animation). Pixel art wants its texture loaded unsmoothed: load_texture(file, false), as every texture is after pixel_art().

Where each puts a picture: the point given is its top left corner or its middle, and `origin` (vec2(0, 0): the top left corner, vec2(0.5, 0.5): the middle, vec2(0.5, 1): the middle of the bottom edge, a character's feet) puts another point of the picture there. An angle turns it around its middle.

```gdscript
sprite(t, pos)                        pos: its top left corner (origin = vec2(0, 0))
sprite_region(t, src, center, size)   center: its middle (origin = vec2(0.5, 0.5))
draw_frame(sheet, i, center)          its middle
draw_anim(sheet, anim, t, center)     its middle
anim.draw(center)                     its middle (an AnimPlayer)
draw_tile(map, id, center)            its middle
sprite_tiled(t, area)                 fills area (x, y: its top left corner)
draw_target(rt, pos)                  its top left corner
text(s, pos)                          the top left corner; text_centered(s, center): the middle

hero = sprite_sheet(load_texture(embed("hero.png"), false), 32, 32)
walk = animation(4, 7, 10.0)                    # frames 4 to 7, ten a second
draw = ():
    draw_anim(hero, walk, elapsed(), pos, 3.0)  # three times as big
```

An AnimPlayer plays the sheet's animations by name, each from its start when it changes:

```gdscript
hero.anims["idle"] = animation(0, 1, 2.0)
hero.anims["run"] = animation(2, 5, 12.0)
anim = anim_player(hero)
...in update: anim.play(if moving: "run" else: "idle")   # (the same one again goes on)
...in draw:   anim.draw(feet, origin = vec2(0.5, 1), flip_x = facing < 0.0)
```

- `struct Animation`
  ```gdscript
  struct Animation:
      frames: int[]               # the sheet's frames, in order
      durations: f64[]            # how long each one shows (seconds)
      looping = true
  ```
- `struct SpriteSheet`
  ```gdscript
  struct SpriteSheet:
      texture: Texture
      frames: vec4[]              # each frame's rectangle in the texture: x, y, width, height
      anims: {str: Animation}     # its animations by name (load_aseprite: its tags)
  ```
- `sprite_sheet = (t: Texture, frame_w: int, frame_h: int, count: int = 0, spacing: int = 0, margin: int = 0) -> SpriteSheet`: a sheet of equal frames in a grid, numbered from 0 left to right, then down (count: how many there are, if the last row is not full; spacing: pixels between frames; margin: around them)
- `animation = (frames: int[], fps: f64 = 10.0, looping: bool = true) -> Animation`: an animation through these frames, `fps` a second
- `animation = (first: int, last: int, fps: f64 = 10.0, looping: bool = true) -> Animation`: frames first..last (inclusive)
- `anim_length = (a: Animation) -> f64`: how long it takes to go through once (seconds)
- `anim_frame = (a: Animation, t: f64) -> int`: the frame showing t seconds after it started (a non-looping one stays on its last frame)
- `anim_done = (a: Animation, t: f64) -> bool`: has a non-looping animation finished, t seconds after it started?
- `draw_frame = (s: SpriteSheet, frame: int, center: vec2, scale: f64 = 1.0, tint: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), angle: f64 = 0.0, flip_x: bool = false, flip_y: bool = false, origin: vec2 = vec2(0.5, 0.5))`: frame i of the sheet, its middle at `center` (scale: its size times this; flip_x: mirrored, to face the other way; origin: the point of it at center, vec2(0.5, 1) its bottom edge's middle)
- `draw_anim = (s: SpriteSheet, a: Animation, t: f64, center: vec2, scale: f64 = 1.0, tint: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), angle: f64 = 0.0, flip_x: bool = false, flip_y: bool = false, origin: vec2 = vec2(0.5, 0.5))`: the frame an animation shows t seconds after it started
- `draw_anim = (s: SpriteSheet, name: str, t: f64, center: vec2, scale: f64 = 1.0, tint: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), angle: f64 = 0.0, flip_x: bool = false, flip_y: bool = false, origin: vec2 = vec2(0.5, 0.5))`: the same, with the sheet's animation of this name (sheet.anims: Aseprite's tags, or set with `sheet.anims["run"] = animation(2, 5)`)
- `struct AnimPlayer`: plays a sheet's animations by name: the one playing, and when it started. Changing to another starts that one from its first frame; play with the one playing goes on with it. Its clock is elapsed(), so it goes on while a game's own pause screen shows (draw_anim(sheet, p.name, t, ...) draws it on a clock of the game's own).
  ```gdscript
  struct AnimPlayer:
      sheet: SpriteSheet
      name = ""                   # the animation playing ("": none yet)
      start = 0.0                 # when it started (elapsed())
  ```
- `anim_player = (sheet: SpriteSheet, name: str = "") -> AnimPlayer`
- `play = (p: mut AnimPlayer, name: str, restart: bool = false)`: play the sheet's animation of this name: from its start if another was playing (or restart: even if it was this one)
- `anim_time = (p: AnimPlayer) -> f64`: how long the animation playing has played (seconds)
- `anim_frame = (p: AnimPlayer) -> int`: the frame it shows now
- `anim_done = (p: AnimPlayer) -> bool`: has a non-looping animation played to its end (an attack, a jump's take-off)?
- `draw = (p: AnimPlayer, center: vec2, scale: f64 = 1.0, tint: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), angle: f64 = 0.0, flip_x: bool = false, flip_y: bool = false, origin: vec2 = vec2(0.5, 0.5))`: draw the frame it shows now, as draw_frame does
- `load_aseprite = (t: Texture, json: str) -> SpriteSheet \| JsonError`: a sheet from Aseprite's export: its texture, and the JSON it wrote beside it (as an array or a hash of frames). Tags become animations (forward, reverse or ping-pong), with each frame's own duration.

### tilemap.jo

Tilemaps: layers of tiles drawn from a tileset image. Make one from text (tile_layer: each character is a tile), or load a map made in Tiled (`load_tiled("level.tmx", embed_dir("assets"))`: .tmx or .tmj, with its tilesets and their images found by their paths). Draw it with draw_tilemap, and move boxes through it with tilemap_move, which stops them at solid tiles. Every tile is solid unless set_tile_shape says otherwise: a one-way platform (landed on from above, passed through from below and the sides), a slope (a floor that rises or falls across the tile), or empty (scenery). Past the map's edges there is nothing (a box walks off its side and falls) unless `edges` says there are walls. Tiles can have properties of any name (`hurts`, `coin`), set in Tiled or with set_tile_prop, and tilemap_touches asks whether a box touches one (hazards); tilemap_tiles says which tiles a box is in.

```gdscript
tiles = tileset(load_texture(embed("tiles.png"), false), 16, 16)
level = tilemap(tiles, [tile_layer("walls", WALLS, "#=")])     # '#': tile 1, '=': tile 2
update = (dt: f64):
    m = tilemap_move(level, 0, box, vel * f32(dt))
    box = m.rect
    if m.hit_y: vel.y = 0.0
draw = (): draw_tilemap(level)
```

In Tiled, a tile's custom property `shape` (a string) is its shape for tilemap_move, as set_tile_shape would set it: `solid`, `empty`, `platform`, or `slope 0 1` (its floor's height at the tile's left and right sides, 0 to 1). Every other custom property of a tile is kept as text: `tile_prop(level, id, "hurts")` is "true" for a bool property ticked. The map's own custom properties are `level.props`, a layer's `layer.props`, an object's `o.props`. A tile object (Insert Tile) has its tile in `o.tile` and its rect from its top left corner, as every object's.

- `struct Tileset`
  ```gdscript
  struct Tileset:
      texture: Texture
      tile_w: int
      tile_h: int
      columns: int
      first = 1                   # the id of its first tile (Tiled's firstgid)
      count = 0                   # tiles in it
      spacing = 0                 # pixels between tiles in the image
      margin = 0                  # around them
  ```
- `struct TileLayer`
  ```gdscript
  struct TileLayer:
      name: str
      width: int                  # in tiles
      height: int
      tiles: int[]                # tile ids, a row at a time from the top; 0: no tile
      visible = true
      offset = vec2(0, 0)         # drawn this far from the map's corner (Tiled's layer offset; tile_at and tilemap_move do not move with it)
      parallax = vec2(1, 1)       # how fast it scrolls with the camera: 1 with the map, 0.5 half as fast (far away), 0 not at all
      opacity = 1.0
      props: {str: str}           # its custom properties in Tiled, as text
      image: Texture? = none      # an image layer's picture (Tiled's image layer: no tiles), its top left corner at offset
      repeat_x = false            # (an image layer: repeated across, down)
      repeat_y = false
  ```
- `struct MapObject`: an object placed in Tiled (an object layer's): a spawn point, a door, a coin...
  ```gdscript
  struct MapObject:
      name: str
      kind: str                   # its class (Tiled's "type")
      layer: str                  # the object layer it is on
      rect: Rect                  # where (a point: width and height 0); a tile object's too, top left
      props: {str: str}           # its custom properties, as text
      tile = 0                    # a tile object's tile (Tiled's Insert Tile), an id as tile_at gives; 0: not one
      flip_x = false              # (a tile object: drawn mirrored)
      flip_y = false
      id = 0                      # its id in Tiled (what an object property refers to)
      rotation = 0.0              # degrees, clockwise (around its top left corner; a tile object's, its bottom left)
  ```
- `enum TileShape:`: how a tile stops what moves through the map (tilemap_move): solid; empty (scenery); platform (one-way: landed on from above, passed through from below and the sides); slope(left, right), a floor whose height (0: the tile's bottom, 1: its top) goes from left at the tile's left side to right at its right side: slope(0, 1) rises to the right, slope(0, 0.5) then slope(0.5, 1) gently over two tiles (passed through from below)
  ```gdscript
  enum TileShape:
      solid
      empty
      platform
      slope(left: f64, right: f64)
  ```
- `enum MapEdges: open, sides, closed`: what is past the map's edges, for tilemap_move: nothing (open: a box walks off the side and falls); walls at its left and right sides (sides: from far above to far below it); or walls all round (closed)
- `struct Tilemap`
  ```gdscript
  struct Tilemap:
      tile_w: int
      tile_h: int
      width: int                  # in tiles
      height: int
      tilesets: Tileset[]
      layers: TileLayer[]
      objects: MapObject[]
      shapes: TileShape[]         # by tile id (set_tile_shape); solid past its end
      props: {str: str}           # the map's custom properties in Tiled, as text
      tile_props: {int: {str: str}}   # each tile's custom properties, by tile id (tile_prop, set_tile_prop)
      tile_anims: {int: Animation}    # a tile's animation (Tiled's tile animations): its frames are tile ids
      edges = MapEdges.open       # tilemap_move: solid walls at the map's edges (`level.edges = .sides`)
      parallax_origin = vec2(0, 0)    # where the view's middle is when layers with a parallax are where their offset puts them
  ```
- `struct TileCell`: a tile a box touches (tilemap_tiles): its column, its row, and its id
  ```gdscript
  struct TileCell:
      x: int
      y: int
      id: int
  ```
- `struct TileMove`
  ```gdscript
  struct TileMove:
      rect: Rect                  # where the box ended up
      hit_x: bool                 # it was stopped going sideways
      hit_y: bool                 # ... or up or down (moving down: it landed)
      platform = -1               # the one of `platforms` it stands on after the move (-1: none)
      solid = -1                  # ... of `solids`
  ```
- `tileset = (t: Texture, tile_w: int, tile_h: int, spacing: int = 0, margin: int = 0) -> Tileset`: a tileset: an image of equal tiles in a grid, numbered from `first` (1) left to right, then down
- `tile_layer = (name: str, rows: str, chars: str) -> TileLayer`: a layer from rows of text: the character at position i of `chars` is tile i + 1, and any other character (a space, a dot) is no tile; a character the rows never use skips a number ("_#": '#' is tile 2). Rows of spaces are rows of the map (open sky); only an empty first line (the line break after the opening quote) and a last line of spaces (before the closing quote) are left out. For tilemap_move every tile is solid, floor tiles too: a tile walked over is `set_tile_shape(map, id, .empty)`, or a character left out of `chars` (no tile).
- `tilemap = (ts: Tileset, layers: TileLayer[]) -> Tilemap`: a map of these layers, drawn with one tileset
- `layer_index = (m: Tilemap, name: str) -> int`: the index of the layer with this name (-1: none)
- `tile_at = (m: Tilemap, layer: int, x: int, y: int) -> int`: the tile at column x, row y of a layer (0 outside the map)
- `tile_at = (m: Tilemap, layer: int, p: vec2) -> int`: the tile under a point (in pixels)
- `set_tile = (m: mut Tilemap, layer: int, x: int, y: int, id: int)`
- `set_tile_shape = (m: mut Tilemap, id: int, shape: TileShape)`: how tile id stops what moves (every tile is solid until this says otherwise)
- `tile_shape = (m: Tilemap, id: int) -> TileShape`
- `tilemap_size = (m: Tilemap) -> vec2`: the map's size in pixels
- `draw_tilemap = (m: Tilemap, layer: int = -1, at: vec2 = vec2(0, 0), tint: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1))`: draw a layer (-1: every visible layer, bottom first), with the map's top left corner at `at`. Only the tiles on the screen are drawn, wherever the camera is. A layer is drawn at its offset, and one with a parallax other than 1 moves with the camera at that fraction of its speed (as Tiled shows it: where the view's middle is at the map's parallax_origin, at its offset); an image layer draws its picture there (repeated, if it repeats); tiles with an animation in Tiled go through its frames.
- `draw_tile = (m: Tilemap, id: int, center: vec2, size: vec2 = vec2(0, 0), tint: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1))`: draw tile id (from the map's tilesets; Tiled's flip bits mirror it), its middle at `center`, its size the map's tiles' (or size): a tile object (`draw_tile(level, o.tile, center(o.rect), vec2(o.rect.w, o.rect.h))`), a tile picked up, an editor's palette
- `tilemap_hits = (m: Tilemap, layer: int, r: Rect) -> bool`: does a box (in pixels) touch a tile of the layer? Any tile, whatever its shape: an .empty one (scenery, spikes) too; tilemap_touches asks for tiles with a property, tilemap_tiles says which tiles they are
- `tilemap_tiles = (m: Tilemap, layer: int, r: Rect) -> TileCell[]`: the tiles of the layer a box touches (no empty cells), row by row from the top: what a player stands in, `loop c in tilemap_tiles(level, 0, box): if tile_prop(level, c.id, "coin") != none: set_tile(level, 0, c.x, c.y, 0)`
- `tilemap_touches = (m: Tilemap, layer: int, r: Rect, prop: str) -> bool`: does a box touch a tile with this custom property (set in Tiled, or by set_tile_prop), one not "false", "0" or empty? Hazards: `if tilemap_touches(level, 0, box.grow(-2), "hurts"): die()`
- `tilemap_touches = (m: Tilemap, layer: int, r: Rect, id: int) -> bool`: does a box touch tile id?
- `tile_prop = (m: Tilemap, id: int, name: str) -> str?`: a custom property of tile id, as text (a Tiled tile's, or set_tile_prop's); none: it has none of that name
- `set_tile_prop = (m: mut Tilemap, id: int, name: str, value: str)`
- `tilemap_move = (m: Tilemap, layer: int, r: Rect, d: vec2, drop: bool = false, platforms: Rect[] = [], solids: Rect[] = []) -> TileMove`: move a box by d, stopping it against the layer's tiles: sideways first, then up or down, so it slides along walls and floors. However far it moves in one step, it does not pass through a solid tile. Platforms stop it only coming down onto them (drop: not even then, to drop through one); on a slope it walks up and down the slope's floor, staying on it going down. Past the map's edges there is nothing, unless its `edges` say there are walls (`level.edges = .sides`): a box walks off the side and falls. Boxes that are not tiles stop it too: `platforms` (one-way, as platform tiles: moving platforms, landed on from above) and `solids` (stopping it from every side: a crate, a door); the result's `platform` (or `solid`) says which one it stands on after the move, for a moving one to carry it in the next frame (see "Platformers" in the docs).

#### Tiled

- `load_tiled = (path: str, files: {str: u8[]}) -> Tilemap \| JsonError`: a map made in Tiled, with its tilesets and their images, from files built into the program: The map may be Tiled's XML (.tmx) or JSON (.tmj), its tilesets inside it or in files of their own (.tsx, .tsj), found as Tiled does: next to the file that names them. Tile layers (also inside groups) become layers, with their offsets, parallax factors, opacity and properties (a group's passed on to its layers); image layers become layers with a picture (`image`, drawn by draw_tilemap, repeated if Tiled repeats it); object layers become `objects`; tile animations play in draw_tilemap; tiles stay sharp (no smoothing). Not read: infinite maps, maps that are not orthogonal, tilesets made of separate images, a layer's tint color, Wang sets.
  ```gdscript
  assets = embed_dir("assets")
  level = load_tiled("maps/level1.tmx", assets)
  ```
- `load_tiled = (text: str, textures: Texture[]) -> Tilemap \| JsonError`: a map saved by Tiled (its text: XML, .tmx, or JSON, .tmj), with its tilesets embedded in it (Tiled: in the Tilesets view, "Embed Tileset"), and a texture for each tileset, in the map's order (load_tiled(path, files) finds tileset files and images by itself). Layer data can be plain, CSV or base64 (uncompressed, zlib or gzip). Image layers are layers with no picture here (load_tiled(path, files) loads theirs).

## Platformers

How the pieces fit for a platformer (the program here is one): `tilemap_move` for running and landing (one-way platforms, slopes, `drop` to fall through a platform), `level.edges = .sides` for walls at a level's ends, `platforms` (and `solids`) for moving platforms (move them first, then carry what stood on one by what it moved: the move's `platform` says which), hazards as a tile's property (`tilemap_touches(level, 0, box, "hurts")`; in Tiled, a custom property of the tile), a wall slide from `hit_x`, coyote time and a jump buffer as two timers, a camera held inside the level with `clamp`, and `fixed_update` for the same jumps at any frame rate. `sloppy new mygame platformer` starts a project with slopes, coins and a camera; `AnimPlayer` ([sprites](#spritesjo)) plays a character's animations by name, and `sprite_tiled` repeats a background as the camera moves.

```gdscript
# example: platformers
# a level with spikes, walls at its sides, and a platform that carries the player across a pit;
# running, jumping (with coyote time and a jump buffer), sliding down walls and jumping off them,
# and a camera that stays inside the level. Arrows or A/D to run, space to jump.
window("Platformer", 960, 540)
const T = 32

LEVEL = r"
......................................
......................................
......................................
......................................
......................................
......................................
......................................
......................................
......................................
...............................o......
.............................=====....
...........o..........................
..........===.....................#...
..........................#.......#...
...@......................#.......#...
########............#######^^^^####...
########^^^^^^^^^^^^##################
"

# the tiles: ground, a one-way platform, spikes (drawn here; a tileset image does as well)
img = image(3 * T, T)
loop y in 0..T:
    loop x in 0..T:
        img.set(x, y, rgb(0.42, 0.3, 0.22))
        if y < 8: img.set(T + x, y, rgb(0.7, 0.5, 0.3))
        if y > T - 2 * abs(x % 16 - 8) * 2: img.set(2 * T + x, y, rgb(0.85, 0.25, 0.25))
level = tilemap(tileset(texture(img), T, T), [tile_layer("ground", LEVEL, "#=^")])
set_tile_shape(level, 2, .platform)
set_tile_shape(level, 3, .empty)            # spikes: passed into, not stood on...
set_tile_prop(level, 3, "hurts", "true")    # ...and they hurt (in Tiled: a custom property of the tile)
level.edges = .sides                        # walls at the level's left and right ends

# a moving platform: where it is, and how far it moved in the last step (to carry its rider)
struct Mover:
    rect: Rect
    from: vec2
    to: vec2
    t = 0.0
    moved = vec2(0, 0)
movers = [Mover(Rect(8 * T, 15 * T, 3 * T, 10), vec2(8 * T, 15 * T), vec2(17 * T, 15 * T))]

const RUN = 300.0
const JUMP = 760.0
const GRAVITY = 2200.0
start = vec2(3 * T, 14 * T)
box = Rect(start.x, start.y, 22, 30)
vel = vec2(0, 0)
on_ground = false
coyote = 0.0        # time left to jump after running off a ledge
buffered = 0.0      # time left for a jump pressed just before landing
wall = 0.0          # -1: a wall on the left is touched, 1: on the right
riding = -1         # the mover stood on
cam = start
action("jump", [.key(.space), .key(.up), .button(.a)])

# the game in steps of 1/60 s, whatever the frame rate (a press is seen in one step only)
update = (dt: f64): fixed_update(dt, step)

step = (dt: f64):
    global box, buffered, cam, coyote, on_ground, riding, vel, wall
    # the movers move first, and carry what stands on them
    loop mut mv in movers:
        mv.t += dt
        p = mix(mv.from, mv.to, f32(0.5 - 0.5 * cos(mv.t * 0.8)))
        mv.moved = p - vec2(mv.rect.x, mv.rect.y)
        mv.rect.x = p.x
        mv.rect.y = p.y
    if riding >= 0: box = tilemap_move(level, 0, box, movers[riding].moved).rect
    move = input_axis().x
    vel.x = approach(vel.x, move * RUN, 14.0, dt)
    vel.y += GRAVITY * dt
    if vel.y < 0.0 and not action_down("jump"): vel.y += GRAVITY * dt      # let go early: a short hop
    # sliding down a wall pushed into
    if wall != 0.0 and move == wall and not on_ground: vel.y = min(vel.y, 120.0)
    coyote -= dt
    buffered -= dt
    if on_ground: coyote = 0.1
    if action_pressed("jump"): buffered = 0.12
    if buffered > 0.0 and (coyote > 0.0 or wall != 0.0):
        if coyote <= 0.0: vel.x = -wall * RUN          # off a wall: away from it
        vel.y = -JUMP
        buffered = 0.0
        coyote = 0.0
    hit = tilemap_move(level, 0, box, vel * f32(dt), drop = input_axis().y > 0.5, platforms = movers.map((mv): mv.rect))
    box = hit.rect
    wall = 0.0
    if hit.hit_x:
        wall = sign(vel.x)
        vel.x = 0.0
    on_ground = hit.hit_y and vel.y > 0.0
    if hit.hit_y: vel.y = 0.0
    riding = hit.platform
    # spikes, or a fall out of the level: back to the start
    if tilemap_touches(level, 0, box.grow(-4), "hurts") or box.y > tilemap_size(level).y:
        box = Rect(start.x, start.y, box.w, box.h)
        vel = vec2(0, 0)
    # the camera follows, and stops at the level's edges
    size = tilemap_size(level)
    cam = follow(cam, center(box), dt, 6.0, vec2(80, 60))
    cam = clamp(cam, vec2(480, 270), vec2(max(size.x - 480.0, 480.0), max(size.y - 270.0, 270.0)))

draw = ():
    clear(rgb(0.5, 0.72, 0.92))
    camera2d(cam)
    draw_tilemap(level)
    loop mv in movers: rect(mv.rect, rgb(0.55, 0.4, 0.3))
    rect(box, rgb(0.95, 0.85, 0.3))
```

## Colors, vectors and matrices

`vec2`, `vec3`, `vec4`, `ivec2` and `mat4` are built-in types, with `+ - *` and fields `.x .y .z .w` (and swizzles: `v.xz`). Colors are `vec4`s: red, green, blue and alpha, from 0 to 1.

```gdscript
# example: colors, vectors and matrices
a = vec2(3, 4)
b = vec2(1, 0)
print(length(a), normalize(a), dot(a, b), a + b * 2.0, a.x)
print(distance(a, b), lerp(a, b, 0.5), angle(a), from_angle(PI / 2.0))

v = vec3(1, 2, 3)
print(cross(vec3(1, 0, 0), vec3(0, 1, 0)), v.xz, length(v))

m = translation(vec3(10, 0, 0)) * rotation_y(PI / 2.0)    # turn, then move
print(transform_point(m, vec3(1, 0, 0)))                   # about (10, 0, -1)

# colors are vec4s: red, green, blue, alpha from 0 to 1
print(rgb(1, 0.5, 0), hex(0x3399ff), hsv(0.33, 1.0, 1.0), with_alpha(RED, 0.5))
```

Vector and matrix math for the built-in vec2/vec3/vec4/mat4 types (f32 components). mat4 is column-major: m[i] is column i.

- `dot = (a: vec2, b: vec2) -> f32`
- `dot = (a: vec3, b: vec3) -> f32`
- `dot = (a: vec4, b: vec4) -> f32`
- `cross = (a: vec3, b: vec3) -> vec3`
- `cross = (a: vec2, b: vec2) -> f32`
- `length = (v: vec2) -> f32`
- `length = (v: vec3) -> f32`
- `length = (v: vec4) -> f32`
- `length_sq = (v: vec2) -> f32`
- `length_sq = (v: vec3) -> f32`
- `distance = (a: vec2, b: vec2) -> f32`
- `distance = (a: vec3, b: vec3) -> f32`
- `normalize = (v: vec2) -> vec2`
- `normalize = (v: vec3) -> vec3`
- `normalize = (v: vec4) -> vec4`
- `lerp = (a: f32, b: f32, t: f32) -> f32`
- `lerp = (a: vec2, b: vec2, t: f32) -> vec2`
- `lerp = (a: vec3, b: vec3, t: f32) -> vec3`
- `lerp = (a: vec4, b: vec4, t: f32) -> vec4`
- `mix = (a: f32, b: f32, t: f32) -> f32`
- `mix = (a: vec2, b: vec2, t: f32) -> vec2`
- `mix = (a: vec3, b: vec3, t: f32) -> vec3`
- `mix = (a: vec4, b: vec4, t: f32) -> vec4`
- `abs = (v: vec2) -> vec2`
- `abs = (v: vec3) -> vec3`
- `min = (a: vec2, b: vec2) -> vec2`
- `max = (a: vec2, b: vec2) -> vec2`
- `min = (a: vec3, b: vec3) -> vec3`
- `max = (a: vec3, b: vec3) -> vec3`
- `clamp = (v: vec2, lo: vec2, hi: vec2) -> vec2`
- `clamp = (v: vec3, lo: f32, hi: f32) -> vec3`
- `clamp = (v: vec3, lo: vec3, hi: vec3) -> vec3`
- `floor = (v: vec2) -> vec2`
- `sin = (v: vec2) -> vec2`: GLSL's math on each part of a vector, as a shader writes it (there, GLSL's own)
- `cos = (v: vec2) -> vec2`
- `ceil = (v: vec2) -> vec2`
- `pow = (v: vec2, e: vec2) -> vec2`
- `step = (edge: vec2, x: vec2) -> vec2`
- `step = (edge: f32, x: vec2) -> vec2`
- `smoothstep = (e0: vec2, e1: vec2, x: vec2) -> vec2`
- `smoothstep = (e0: f32, e1: f32, x: vec2) -> vec2`
- `mix = (a: vec2, b: vec2, t: vec2) -> vec2`
- `sin = (v: vec3) -> vec3`
- `cos = (v: vec3) -> vec3`
- `floor = (v: vec3) -> vec3`
- `fract = (v: vec3) -> vec3`
- `ceil = (v: vec3) -> vec3`
- `pow = (v: vec3, e: vec3) -> vec3`
- `step = (edge: vec3, x: vec3) -> vec3`
- `step = (edge: f32, x: vec3) -> vec3`
- `smoothstep = (e0: vec3, e1: vec3, x: vec3) -> vec3`
- `smoothstep = (e0: f32, e1: f32, x: vec3) -> vec3`
- `mix = (a: vec3, b: vec3, t: vec3) -> vec3`
- `sin = (v: vec4) -> vec4`
- `cos = (v: vec4) -> vec4`
- `floor = (v: vec4) -> vec4`
- `fract = (v: vec4) -> vec4`
- `ceil = (v: vec4) -> vec4`
- `abs = (v: vec4) -> vec4`
- `min = (a: vec4, b: vec4) -> vec4`
- `max = (a: vec4, b: vec4) -> vec4`
- `pow = (v: vec4, e: vec4) -> vec4`
- `step = (edge: vec4, x: vec4) -> vec4`
- `step = (edge: f32, x: vec4) -> vec4`
- `smoothstep = (e0: vec4, e1: vec4, x: vec4) -> vec4`
- `smoothstep = (e0: f32, e1: f32, x: vec4) -> vec4`
- `mix = (a: vec4, b: vec4, t: vec4) -> vec4`
- `smoothstep = (e0: f32, e1: f32, x: f32) -> f32`
- `reflect = (d: vec3, n: vec3) -> vec3`
- `reflect = (d: vec2, n: vec2) -> vec2`
- `perp = (v: vec2) -> vec2`
- `angle = (v: vec2) -> f64`
- `from_angle = (a: f64) -> vec2`
- `rotate = (v: vec2, a: f64) -> vec2`
- `move_toward = (a: vec2, b: vec2, step: f32) -> vec2`: a moved toward b by at most step (reaching it, not passing it): a steady speed, where approach eases in
- `move_toward = (a: vec3, b: vec3, step: f64) -> vec3`
- `move_toward = (a: f64, b: f64, step: f64) -> f64`
- `sinf = (x: f32) -> f32`
- `cosf = (x: f32) -> f32`

#### Random points, directions and colors

- `random_direction = () -> vec2`: a direction at a random angle (length 1)
- `random_direction_3d = () -> vec3`: a direction in 3D, all equally likely (length 1)
- `random_point = (low: vec2, high: vec2) -> vec2`: a point in the rectangle between corners low and high
- `random_in_circle = (center: vec2, radius: f64) -> vec2`: a point inside the circle around center (spread evenly over it)
- `random_color = (saturation: f64 = 0.7, value: f64 = 1.0) -> vec4`: a color of a random hue (less saturation: paler; less value: darker)

#### Colors

- `rgb = (r: f32, g: f32, b: f32) -> vec4`
- `rgba = (r: f32, g: f32, b: f32, a: f32) -> vec4`
- `gray = (v: f32) -> vec4`
- `hex = (c: int) -> vec4`: a color from CSS's hex digits: "#ff8000", "#f80", "#ff800080" (with alpha); white if it is not one
- `hex = (s: str) -> vec4`
- `hsv = (h: f64, s: f64, v: f64) -> vec4`
- `with_alpha = (c: vec4, a: f32) -> vec4`
- `lighten = (c: vec4, k: f32) -> vec4`
- `darken = (c: vec4, k: f32) -> vec4`
- `const WHITE = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1)`
- `const BLACK = vec4(0, 0, 0, 1)`
- `const RED = vec4(0.9, 0.2, 0.2, 1)`
- `const GREEN = vec4(0.2, 0.8, 0.3, 1)`
- `const BLUE = vec4(0.2, 0.4, 0.9, 1)`
- `const YELLOW = vec4(1, 0.85, 0.2, 1)`
- `const ORANGE = vec4(1, 0.55, 0.15, 1)`
- `const PURPLE = vec4(0.6, 0.3, 0.8, 1)`
- `const CYAN = vec4(0.2, 0.85, 0.9, 1)`
- `const MAGENTA = vec4(0.9, 0.25, 0.75, 1)`
- `const GRAY = vec4(0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 1)`
- `const TRANSPARENT = vec4(0, 0, 0, 0)`

#### Matrices

- `mat4_identity = () -> mat4`
- `translation = (t: vec3) -> mat4`
- `scaling = (s: vec3) -> mat4`: the same in every direction
- `scaling = (s: f64) -> mat4`
- `rotation = (axis0: vec3, a: f64) -> mat4`
- `rotation_x = (a: f64) -> mat4`
- `rotation_y = (a: f64) -> mat4`
- `rotation_z = (a: f64) -> mat4`
- `facing = (dir: vec3, up: vec3 = vec3(0, 1, 0)) -> mat4`: the turn that points a model's front (-z, the way a camera looks) along dir, its top toward up: a turret aimed at its target, a beam from a to b (`translation(a) * facing(b - a)`)
- `perspective = (fov_y: f64, aspect: f64, near: f64, far: f64) -> mat4`
- `orthographic = (left: f64, right: f64, bottom: f64, top: f64, near: f64, far: f64) -> mat4`
- `look_at = (eye: vec3, target: vec3, up: vec3) -> mat4`
- `transpose = (m: mat4) -> mat4`
- `transform_point = (m: mat4, p: vec3) -> vec3`
- `transform_dir = (m: mat4, d: vec3) -> vec3`
- `inverse = (m: mat4) -> mat4`

#### Quaternions

- `quat_axis_angle = (axis: vec3, a: f64) -> vec4`
- `quat_euler = (pitch: f64, yaw: f64, roll: f64) -> vec4`: turned by pitch about x, then yaw about y, roll about z (as Unity's Euler angles: roll first, then pitch, then yaw), in radians (`quat_euler(0, 90deg, 0)`)
- `quat_facing = (dir: vec3, up: vec3 = vec3(0, 1, 0)) -> vec4`: the turn that points -z along dir (facing's, as a quaternion)
- `quat_inverse = (q: vec4) -> vec4`: the turn back
- `quat_from_mat4 = (m0: mat4) -> vec4`: a rotation matrix's turn (its scaling left out)
- `quat_mul = (a: vec4, b: vec4) -> vec4`
- `quat_rotate = (q: vec4, v: vec3) -> vec3`
- `quat_to_mat4 = (q: vec4) -> mat4`
- `slerp = (a: vec4, b0: vec4, t: f32) -> vec4`

## 3D

```gdscript
# example: 3D
window("3D", 960, 540)

cube = cube_mesh(1.0)
ball = sphere_mesh(0.5)
ground = plane_mesh(40.0)
cam = orbit_camera(vec3(0, 0.5, 0), 7.0)      # drag the mouse to turn, scroll to zoom
t = 0.0

update = (dt: f64):
    t += dt
    cam.update(dt)

draw = ():
    sky(vec3(0.25, 0.45, 0.8), vec3(0.75, 0.82, 0.9))
    sun(vec3(-0.5, 1.0, -0.3))                # where the sun is: up and to the left, behind
    shadows(true)
    camera3d(cam)
    draw_mesh(ground, mat4(), rgb(0.45, 0.5, 0.4))
    draw_mesh(cube, translation(vec3(0, 0.5, 0)) * rotation_y(t), rgb(0.9, 0.4, 0.3))
    loop i in 0..6:
        a = i / 6.0 * TAU + t * 0.5
        p = vec3(3 * cos(a), 0.5 + 0.3 * sin(t * 2.0 + i), 3 * sin(a))
        draw_mesh(ball, translation(p), hsv(i / 6.0, 0.6, 1.0), emissive = 0.4)
    text("drag to turn, scroll to zoom", vec2(20, 20), 20)
```

### draw3d.jo

3D rendering: camera, sun + sky lighting, fog, shadows, instancing and post effects. Draw calls made during draw() are recorded and rendered (shadow pass, then the lit pass) before any 2D drawing that follows them, so a HUD can be drawn on top.

Directions: y is up, in meters. A turn by `a` about y (`transform(pos, a)`, `rotation_y(a)`) turns +z toward +x, so something turned by `a` faces vec3(sin(a), 0, cos(a)); glTF models face +z unturned, so a model faces a direction d turned by atan2(d.x, d.z). facing(d) turns -z, a camera's front, to d. The cameras (fly, follow, orbit) share one yaw: 0 looks along -z, more turns left, and the way ahead is vec3(-sin(yaw), 0, -cos(yaw)).

- `struct Vertex3D`
  ```gdscript
  struct Vertex3D:
      pos: vec3
      normal: vec3
      uv: vec2
      color: vec4
  ```
- `struct SkinVertex3D`: a vertex of a skinned mesh: also the 4 joints that bend it (their places in the skin, as numbers) and how much each does
  ```gdscript
  struct SkinVertex3D:
      pos: vec3
      normal: vec3
      uv: vec2
      color: vec4
      joints: vec4
      weights: vec4
  ```
- `struct Instance3D`
  ```gdscript
  struct Instance3D:
      model: mat4
      color: vec4
  ```
- `struct LitParams`
  ```gdscript
  struct LitParams:
      view_proj: mat4
      model: mat4
      shadow_vp: mat4
      color: vec4
      cam_pos: vec3
      sun_dir: vec3
      sun_color: vec3
      sky_color: vec3
      ground_color: vec3
      fog_color: vec3
      fog_density: f32
      fog_height: f32
      emissive: f32
      specular: f32
      use_tex: f32
      shadow_strength: f32
      shadow_texel: f32           # (one texel of the shadow map, across)
      shadow_taps: f32            # (9 on a GPU; 4 in the software renderer)
      time: f32
      wind: f32
      tex: Texture
      shadow_map: Texture
      lights: vec4[8]             # point lights: position and radius
      light_colors: vec4[8]
      light_count: f32
      bones: vec4[192]            # skinned meshes: each joint's matrix, its first three rows
  ```
- `struct LitOut`
  ```gdscript
  struct LitOut:
      pos: vec4
      world: vec3
      normal: vec3
      uv: vec2
      color: vec4
      shadow_pos: vec4
  ```
- `lit_vs = (v: Vertex3D, u: LitParams) -> LitOut`
- `lit_vs_inst = (v: Vertex3D, inst: Instance3D, u: LitParams) -> LitOut`
- `lit_vs_skin = (v: SkinVertex3D, u: LitParams) -> LitOut`: (a skinned vertex: bent by its joints, then placed by the model matrix)
- `shadow_factor = (u: LitParams, sp: vec4, ndl: f32) -> f32`
- `aces = (c: vec3) -> vec3`
- `lit_fs = (i: LitOut, u: LitParams) -> vec4`
- `struct Draw3D`
  ```gdscript
  struct Draw3D:
      mesh: Mesh
      model: mat4
      color: vec4
      tex: int
      emissive: f32
      specular: f32
      instances: Instance3D[]
      instanced: bool
      shadow: bool
      wind: f32
      custom: () -> void
      has_custom: bool
      front: bool = false
      bones: vec4[] = []          # (skinned: the joints' rows for LitParams.bones)
      both_sides = false          # (draw_mesh's double_sided: its backs drawn too)
  ```
- `camera3d = (pos: vec3, target: vec3, fov_degrees: f64 = 60.0, near: f64 = 0.1, far: f64 = 500.0, up: vec3 = vec3(0, 1, 0))`: place the camera; call once per frame before drawing 3D objects. up is the way that is up on the screen (tilt it to roll the picture: a camera behind a plane, banking with it)
- `world_to_screen = (p: vec3) -> vec2?`: where a point in 3D appears on the screen, in 2D drawing units (for a name or a health bar over someone's head); none when it is behind the camera. As the camera was last placed.
- `screen_ray = (p: vec2) -> (vec3, vec3)`: the ray from the camera through a point on the screen (2D units, as mouse_pos): where it starts and its direction (length 1), for picking what the mouse is on (with a physics3d raycast)
- `sun = (direction: vec3, color: vec3 = vec3(1, 0.95, 0.85))`: the sunlight: direction points toward the sun (y > 0: above the horizon), color is its light
- `sky = (top: vec3, horizon: vec3, ground: vec3 = vec3(0.3, 0.27, 0.24))`
- `hide_sky = ()`
- `night_stars = (amount: f64)`
- `fog = (color: vec3, density: f64 = 0.01, height_falloff: f64 = 0.05)`
- `shadows = (on: bool, strength: f64 = 0.8, area: f64 = 30.0)`
- `post_effects = (bloom: f64 = 0.6, threshold: f64 = 0.7, vignette: f64 = 0.35, saturation: f64 = 1.0, tint: vec3 = vec3(1, 1, 1), include_hud: bool = false)`: bloom + color grading for the frame: all of a 2D game; in a 3D one, the 3D and the 2D drawn before it, while 2D drawn after the 3D (a HUD, text) stays as drawn, unless include_hud
- `no_post_effects = ()`
- `point_light = (pos: vec3, color: vec3, radius: f64 = 6.0, intensity: f64 = 1.0)`: a light at a point for this frame (muzzle flashes, glowing shots, lamps): it reaches radius meters, fading out on the way. Up to 8 light a frame (the ones nearest the camera); they cast no shadows.
- `draw_mesh = (m: Mesh, transform: mat4 = mat4(), color: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), texture: Texture = Texture(0, 0, 0), emissive: f64 = 0.0, specular: f64 = 0.3, cast_shadow: bool = true, in_front: bool = false, double_sided: bool = false)`: A color whose alpha is below 1 is see-through, drawn after the solid ones (rgba(1, 0, 0, 0.5); much emissive makes it near white, which hides that). in_front = true: drawn after everything else and over it, casting no shadow (a first-person weapon, which would otherwise poke into walls the player stands against)
- `draw_line3d = (a: vec3, b: vec3, color: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), width: f64 = 0.03)`: a line from a to b, `width` thick: for seeing paths, rays, normals and boxes while making a game
- `draw_grid3d = (size: f64 = 10.0, step: f64 = 1.0, height: f64 = 0.0, color: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 0.35))`: lines on the ground (y = height), `step` apart out to `size` from the center: to see distances
- `draw_mesh_instanced = (m: Mesh, instances: Instance3D[], color: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), emissive: f64 = 0.0, specular: f64 = 0.2, cast_shadow: bool = true, wind: f64 = 0.0)`: many copies of a mesh in one draw call (crowds, forests, particles...)
- `draw_custom = (render: () -> void, transparent: bool = false, additive: bool = false)`: draw with your own shader inside the 3D pass; `render` runs with depth testing on. Use scene_view_proj(), scene_camera(), scene_sun(), scene_shadow_vp(), scene_shadow_map()... transparent = true: run after the sky with alpha blending on and depth writes off; additive = true: like transparent, but colors add up (glows, beams, light shafts)

#### For shaders

- `hash21 = (p: vec2) -> f32`: a number in [0, 1) that looks random, made from the cell a 2D point is in (its whole parts: noise, stars, sparkle in a shader). Integers, so every GPU and the CPU give the same one

#### 3D particles

- `struct Particle3D`: sparks, smoke, dust and magic: soft dots that always face the camera
  ```gdscript
  ps: Particles3D
  ps.gravity = vec3(0, -9.8, 0)
  emit(ps, hit_point, 30, 4.0, rgb(1, 0.6, 0.2))     # in update, when something happens
  ps.update(dt)                                      # in update, every frame
  ps.draw()                                          # in draw, with the 3D scene
  ```
  ```gdscript
  struct Particle3D:
      pos: vec3
      vel: vec3
      life: f32
      max_life: f32
      size: f32
      color: vec4
  ```
- `struct Particles3D`
  ```gdscript
  struct Particles3D:
      items: Particle3D[]
      gravity: vec3
      drag: f32 = 1.0
  ```
- `emit = (ps: mut Particles3D, pos: vec3, count: int, speed: f64, color: vec4, life: f64 = 1.0, size: f64 = 0.15, spread: f64 = PI, direction: vec3 = vec3(0, 1, 0))`: count particles from pos, at up to speed (m/s), within `spread` radians of `direction` (PI: every way), lasting up to `life` seconds, up to `size` meters across
- `update = (ps: mut Particles3D, dt: f64)`
- `draw = (ps: Particles3D, glow: bool = true)`: draw them with the 3D scene: glowing (colors add up: sparks, fire, magic) or not (blended: smoke, dust); they fade and shrink as they age
- `scene_view_proj = () -> mat4`
- `scene_camera = () -> vec3`
- `scene_sun = () -> vec3`
- `scene_sun_color = () -> vec3`
- `scene_sky_color = () -> vec3`
- `scene_ground_color = () -> vec3`
- `scene_fog_color = () -> vec3`
- `scene_fog = () -> vec2`
- `scene_shadow_vp = () -> mat4`
- `scene_shadow_map = () -> Texture`
- `scene_shadow_strength = () -> f32`
- `instance = (transform: mat4, color: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1)) -> Instance3D`
- `transform = (pos: vec3, rot_y: f64 = 0.0, scale: f64 = 1.0) -> mat4`: transform helpers

#### Meshes

- `cube_mesh = (size: f64 = 1.0) -> Mesh`
- `add_box = (vs: mut Vertex3D[], center: vec3, size: vec3, color: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), transform: mat4 = mat4())`: a box: center, and its size along x, y and z
- `add_cylinder = (vs: mut Vertex3D[], base: vec3, radius: f64, height: f64, color: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), segments: int = 16, top_radius: f64 = -1.0, transform: mat4 = mat4())`: a cylinder standing on base (a cone when top_radius is 0)
- `add_sphere = (vs: mut Vertex3D[], center: vec3, radius: f64, color: vec4 = vec4(1, 1, 1, 1), segments: int = 16, rings: int = 8, transform: mat4 = mat4())`: a sphere (segments around, rings from top to bottom: few of each give a faceted look)
- `sphere_mesh = (radius: f64 = 0.5, segments: int = 32, rings: int = 16) -> Mesh`
- `capsule_mesh = (radius: f64 = 0.5, height: f64 = 2.0, segments: int = 24, rings: int = 16) -> Mesh`: a capsule standing on end: `height` from end to end, centered (physics3d's add_capsule and a character are this shape)
- `terrain_mesh = (size: f64, divisions: int, height: (f64, f64) -> f64, color: (f64, f64, f64) -> vec4) -> Mesh`: a grid in the XZ plane, centered on the origin and `size` across (x and z from -size/2 to size/2), in divisions x divisions squares; `height` gives the elevation at (x, z), and `color` each corner's color from (x, y, z). To stand on it with import physics3d: `w.add_mesh(physics3d.terrain_triangles(size, divisions, height))`, the same triangles
- `plane_mesh = (size: f64 = 10.0) -> Mesh`
- `cylinder_mesh = (radius: f64 = 0.5, height: f64 = 1.0, segments: int = 24, top_radius: f64 = -1.0) -> Mesh`: a cylinder standing on its base at y = 0 (the cube, sphere and capsule meshes are centered instead), up to y = height; top_radius 0 makes a cone
- `cone_mesh = (radius: f64 = 0.5, height: f64 = 1.0, segments: int = 24) -> Mesh`
- `custom_mesh = (vertices: Vertex3D[], indices: u32[] = []) -> Mesh`: a mesh of your own: triangles, three vertices each (or three indices each into vertices). A triangle's front is the side its normals point to, and only fronts are drawn (a box seen from inside is clear): a triangle wound the other way (its corners clockwise as seen from its front) is turned around to face where its normals do. With no normals (all zero), corners go counter-clockwise as seen from the front. draw_mesh(..., double_sided = true) draws both sides

### camera3d.jo

Cameras that the player moves (3D): an orbit camera, around a point (to look at a model, a board, a level from above), and a fly camera (to move through a scene). Each is a value the game keeps: update it with the frame's input, then make it the camera.

```gdscript
cam = orbit_camera(vec3(0, 1, 0), 12.0)
update = (dt: f64): update(cam, dt)
draw = ():
    camera3d(cam)
    draw_mesh(...)
```

- `struct OrbitCamera`: Around target, at distance: dragging with the left or right mouse button (or the right stick) turns it, the wheel (or the left stick up and down) brings it closer or further.
  ```gdscript
  struct OrbitCamera:
      target: vec3
      distance: f64 = 10.0
      yaw: f64 = 0.0                 # around the vertical axis, in radians (0: from +z)
      pitch: f64 = 0.4               # above the horizon, in radians
      min_distance: f64 = 1.0
      max_distance: f64 = 200.0
      turn_speed: f64 = 0.006        # radians per pixel the mouse moves
      smooth: f64 = 14.0             # how quickly it follows (0: at once)
      drag_left = true               # a left-button drag turns it (false: only the right, the left left to the game)
      shown_yaw: f64 = 0.0           # (where it is now, on its way to yaw, pitch and distance)
      shown_pitch: f64 = 0.4
      shown_distance: f64 = 10.0
  ```
- `orbit_camera = (target: vec3 = vec3(0, 0, 0), distance: f64 = 10.0, yaw: f64 = 0.0, pitch: f64 = 0.4) -> OrbitCamera`
- `update = (c: mut OrbitCamera, dt: f64)`
- `position = (c: OrbitCamera) -> vec3`: where the camera is
- `camera3d = (c: OrbitCamera, fov_degrees: f64 = 60.0, near: f64 = 0.1, far: f64 = 500.0)`
- `struct FollowCamera`: Behind a target the game moves (a third-person camera): the mouse while it is locked (mouse_lock()) or the right button is held, or the right stick, turns it around the target, and the wheel brings it closer. Walk the player along flat_forward and flat_right, so that ahead is where the camera looks: Its yaw is the other cameras': 0 looks along -z, more turns it left. Behind a vehicle (a chase camera), give update the way it faces, in the same terms: it swings round behind it, smoothly (chase: how quickly), `update(cam, kart.pos, dt, heading = kart.yaw)`.
  ```gdscript
  cam = follow_camera(distance = 6.0)
  update = (dt: f64):
      a = input_axis()
      move = flat_forward(cam) * -a.y + flat_right(cam) * a.x     # (then move the player)
      update(cam, player_pos, dt)
      # kept out of walls: if let h = w.sphere_cast(aim(cam), position(cam), 0.2): pull_in(cam, h.fraction)
  draw = (): camera3d(cam)
  ```
  ```gdscript
  struct FollowCamera:
      target: vec3                   # what it follows (update sets it)
      distance: f64 = 6.0
      height: f64 = 1.5              # how far above the target it aims
      yaw: f64 = 0.0                 # 0: behind the target, looking along -z; more: turned left
      pitch: f64 = 0.35              # looking down from above, in radians
      min_pitch: f64 = -0.3
      max_pitch: f64 = 1.3
      min_distance: f64 = 2.0
      max_distance: f64 = 20.0
      turn_speed: f64 = 0.004        # radians per pixel the mouse moves
      smooth: f64 = 12.0             # how quickly it follows the target (0: at once)
      chase: f64 = 4.0               # how quickly it swings round behind a heading given to update
      reach: f64 = 1.0               # (the share of its distance it may be out this frame: pull_in)
      shown_target: vec3
      shown_reach: f64 = 1.0
      placed = false
  ```
- `follow_camera = (distance: f64 = 6.0, yaw: f64 = 0.0, pitch: f64 = 0.35, height: f64 = 1.5) -> FollowCamera`
- `update = (c: mut FollowCamera, target: vec3, dt: f64, heading: f64? = none)`
- `pull_in = (c: mut FollowCamera, fraction: f64)`: a wall between aim(c) and position(c): only this share of the way out (a cast's fraction)
- `aim = (c: FollowCamera) -> vec3`: the point it looks at (above the target), and where it is
- `position = (c: FollowCamera) -> vec3`
- `flat_forward = (c: FollowCamera) -> vec3`: the way it looks along the ground, and its right (for walking where it looks)
- `flat_right = (c: FollowCamera) -> vec3`
- `camera3d = (c: FollowCamera, fov_degrees: f64 = 60.0, near: f64 = 0.1, far: f64 = 500.0)`
- `struct FlyCamera`: Through the scene: W A S D (or the arrows, or the left stick) move, Space and Q go up and down, Shift is faster; the mouse looks around while the right button is held, or always when the mouse is locked (mouse_lock()), as does the right stick.
  ```gdscript
  struct FlyCamera:
      pos: vec3
      yaw: f64 = 0.0                 # 0: looking toward -z
      pitch: f64 = 0.0
      speed: f64 = 8.0               # units per second (Shift: four times that)
      turn_speed: f64 = 0.004
  ```
- `fly_camera = (pos: vec3, yaw: f64 = 0.0, pitch: f64 = 0.0) -> FlyCamera`
- `forward = (c: FlyCamera) -> vec3`: the way it looks (a unit vector), and its right
- `right = (c: FlyCamera) -> vec3`
- `update = (c: mut FlyCamera, dt: f64)`
- `camera3d = (c: FlyCamera, fov_degrees: f64 = 60.0, near: f64 = 0.1, far: f64 = 500.0)`

## Models (glTF)

3D models from glTF 2.0 files (.glb, or .gltf with its .bin and images), the format Blender and most tools export: meshes, with their materials' base color (a factor and a texture, PNG or JPEG), vertex colors and emissive light, placed by the scene's nodes; skins (meshes bent by a skeleton of nodes, up to 64 joints) and their animations.

```gdscript
ship = load_model(embed("ship.glb"))
draw = ():
    camera3d(...)
    draw_model(ship, translation(pos) * rotation_y(angle))
```

An animated model is drawn in a pose: an animation at a time, or two blended.

```gdscript
run = find_animation(hero, "Run")
draw_model(hero, transform(pos, facing), pose = pose_at(hero, run, elapsed()))
```

A .gltf names its other files: give them by those names, as `files` (or put them in the .gltf as data: URIs). Not read: morph targets, compressed meshes (Draco, meshopt), sparse accessors; materials beyond the base color (metal, roughness, normal maps).

- `struct ModelPart`
  ```gdscript
  struct ModelPart:
      mesh: Mesh
      transform: mat4             # where the scene's nodes put it, in the model
      color: vec4                 # the material's base color (times the vertices' colors)
      texture: Texture            # its base color texture (Texture(0, 0, 0): none)
      emissive: f64               # how much it glows (from the material's emissive color)
      node: int = -1              # the node it hangs from (in Model.nodes)
      skin: int = -1              # the skin that bends it (in Model.skins; -1: none)
  ```
- `struct ModelNode`: a node of the model's scene: its name, its parent (-1: none), and where it rests, from its parent: a translation, a rotation (a quaternion) and a scale
  ```gdscript
  struct ModelNode:
      name: str
      parent: int
      t: vec3
      r: vec4
      s: vec3
  ```
- `struct Skin`: the joints (nodes) that bend a skin's vertices, and the inverse of where each was when the mesh was bound to them
  ```gdscript
  struct Skin:
      joints: int[]
      inverse_bind: mat4[]
  ```
- `struct AnimChannel`: keys moving one node: its translation (path 0), rotation (1) or scale (2) at the times given (3 values a key, 4 for rotations), in steps or blended between keys
  ```gdscript
  struct AnimChannel:
      node: int
      path: int
      step: bool
      times: f32[]
      values: f32[]
  ```
- `struct ModelAnimation`
