Contacts: `w.contacts(b)` after a step, each with `.new` on the step they met; a sensor (`sensor = true`: a pickup, a trigger zone) collides with nothing but is in them too. As in 2D, stepping is the same for the same inputs (for replays and rollback). A small, fast body can pass through a thin wall in one step (there is no continuous collision yet): for bullets, use w.raycast from where they were to where they are.

- `enum BodyKind: dynamic, fixed, moving`
- `enum Shape: none, sphere, box, capsule, triangles`
- `struct Body`: (a handle: a body's place, and which body has had it: a removed body's handle is not taken for the next one put there)
  ```gdscript
  struct Body:
      id: int
      gen: int
  ```
- `struct Joint`
  ```gdscript
  struct Joint:
      id: int
      gen: int
  ```
- `struct Contact`
  ```gdscript
  struct Contact:
      a: Body
      b: Body
      point: vec3
      normal: vec3            # from a toward b
      impulse: f32
      new: bool
  ```
- `struct Hit`
  ```gdscript
  struct Hit:
      body: Body
      point: vec3
      normal: vec3
      fraction: f32
  ```

#### The world

- `struct World`
  ```gdscript
  struct World:
      gravity = vec3(0, -9.81, 0)
      iterations = 10
      sleep = true            # bodies that have come to rest are left out until something touches them
      substep = 1.0 / 120.0
      bodies: __B3[] = [__B3(kind = .fixed)]
      joints: __J3[]
      meshes: __Mesh3[]
      arbs: __Arb3[]
      arb_index: {int: int}
      free: int[]
      events: Contact[]
  ```

#### Making bodies

- `add_sphere = (w: mut World, pos: vec3, radius: f64, kind: BodyKind = .dynamic, density: f64 = 1000, friction: f64 = 0.5, bounce: f64 = 0.0, sensor: bool = false, mass: f64 = 0) -> Body`
- `add_box = (w: mut World, pos: vec3, size: vec3, rot: vec4 = vec4(0, 0, 0, 1), kind: BodyKind = .dynamic, density: f64 = 1000, friction: f64 = 0.5, bounce: f64 = 0.0, sensor: bool = false, mass: f64 = 0) -> Body`: size: the box's full width, height and depth
- `add_capsule = (w: mut World, pos: vec3, radius: f64, height: f64, rot: vec4 = vec4(0, 0, 0, 1), kind: BodyKind = .dynamic, density: f64 = 1000, friction: f64 = 0.5, bounce: f64 = 0.0, sensor: bool = false, mass: f64 = 0) -> Body`: a capsule standing up: height from the bottom to the top of its round ends
- `add_mesh = (w: mut World, triangles: vec3[], friction: f64 = 0.6) -> Body`: fixed triangles (three corners each, in the world): a level, the ground
- `triangles = (md: ModelData) -> vec3[]`: a model's triangles, where its parts are (for add_mesh)
- `terrain_triangles = (size: f64, divisions: int, height: (f64, f64) -> f64) -> vec3[]`: the triangles of terrain_mesh(size, divisions, height, ...), for add_mesh: the ground of the terrain drawn with the same size, divisions and height
- `set_mass = (w: mut World, b: Body, m: f64)`: give a dynamic body another mass, in kilograms (its turning inertia changes with it)
- `remove = (w: mut World, b: Body)`: take b out of the world (and the joints it is in); its handle then means nothing (a handle used after this panics; removing twice does nothing)
- `remove = (w: mut World, j: Joint)`
- `joint_alive = (w: World, j: Joint) -> bool`: the joint is still there (not removed, nor one of its bodies)

#### Asking and changing

- `bodies = (w: World) -> Body[]`
- `shape = (w: World, b: Body) -> Shape`: its shape, and (for drawing it) its size: a box's full width, height and depth, a sphere's or a capsule's width (and a capsule's height, end to end, in y)
- `size = (w: World, b: Body) -> vec3`
- `radius = (w: World, b: Body) -> f64`
- `pos = (w: World, b: Body) -> vec3`
- `rotation = (w: World, b: Body) -> vec4`
- `velocity = (w: World, b: Body) -> vec3`
- `spin = (w: World, b: Body) -> vec3`
- `mass = (w: World, b: Body) -> f64`
- `kind = (w: World, b: Body) -> BodyKind`
- `alive = (w: World, b: Body) -> bool`
- `tag = (w: World, b: Body) -> int`
- `set_tag = (w: mut World, b: Body, t: int)`
- `transform = (w: World, b: Body) -> mat4`: where and how it is turned, as a matrix for drawing (a box's or capsule's size not included)
- `to_world = (w: World, b: Body, local: vec3) -> vec3`
- `to_local = (w: World, b: Body, p: vec3) -> vec3`
- `set_pos = (w: mut World, b: Body, p: vec3)`
- `set_rotation = (w: mut World, b: Body, q: vec4)`
- `set_velocity = (w: mut World, b: Body, v: vec3)`
- `set_spin = (w: mut World, b: Body, s: vec3)`
- `sleeping = (w: World, b: Body) -> bool`: it is asleep: at rest with all it touches, left out of each step until something touches it
- `set_damping = (w: mut World, b: Body, d: f64)`
- `set_fixed_rotation = (w: mut World, b: Body, on: bool)`: it never turns, whatever hits it (an upright enemy, a character's body)
- `set_friction = (w: mut World, b: Body, f: f64)`
- `set_bounce = (w: mut World, b: Body, f: f64)`
- `set_layers = (w: mut World, b: Body, layer: int, mask: int = -1)`: which layer a body is on (a bit: 1, 2, 4, 8...; every body starts on 1) and which layers it touches (mask: bits or'ed together; -1, the start: all). Two bodies touch when each one's layer is in the other's mask; rays, casts and overlaps take a mask too. For a collider only the camera sees: `w.set_layers(crown, 4)` and `w.sphere_cast(a, b, r, mask = 1 | 4)`, the player kept out of it with `player.mask = 1 | 2` (a character's mask is its own)
- `push = (w: mut World, b: Body, impulse: vec3)`
- `push = (w: mut World, b: Body, impulse: vec3, at: vec3)`
- `apply_force = (w: mut World, b: Body, f: vec3)`
- `apply_torque = (w: mut World, b: Body, t: vec3)`

#### Joints

- `ball = (w: mut World, a: Body, b: Body, at: vec3) -> Joint`: a ball and socket at the point at (in the world, now)
- `hinge = (w: mut World, a: Body, b: Body, at: vec3, axis: vec3, lower: f64 = -1e9, upper: f64 = 1e9, motor_speed: f64 = 0, max_torque: f64 = 0) -> Joint`: a hinge at at, turning about axis (in the world, now); lower, upper: how far b may turn against a (radians, from how they are now; about axis, by the right hand); motor_speed: b turned against a at that many radians a second, with up to max_torque (0: no motor)
- `set_motor = (w: mut World, j: Joint, speed: f64, max_torque: f64)`: change a hinge's motor: b turns against a at speed radians a second, with up to max_torque (0: no motor)
- `set_limits = (w: mut World, j: Joint, lower: f64, upper: f64)`: change how far a hinge lets b turn against a: from lower to upper radians
- `joint_angle = (w: World, j: Joint) -> f64`: how far b has turned against a since the joint was made, in radians
- `rod = (w: mut World, a: Body, b: Body, at_a: vec3, at_b: vec3, spring: f64 = 0, damping: f64 = 0.5, rope: bool = false) -> Joint`: the points at_a of a and at_b of b kept as far apart as they are (spring: a spring of that many oscillations a second; rope: only as far at most)
- `ends = (w: World, j: Joint) -> (vec3, vec3)`

#### Stepping

- `step = (w: mut World, dt: f64)`
- `contacts = (w: World) -> Contact[]`
- `contacts = (w: World, b: Body) -> Contact[]`

#### Overlaps

- `overlap_sphere = (w: World, center: vec3, radius: f64, mask: int = -1) -> Body[]`: the bodies (sensors too) a sphere, a box or a character overlaps: the trigger zones and pickups a character is in (it is not a body, so it makes no contacts), what an explosion reaches
  ```gdscript
  loop b in w.overlaps(hero): if w.tag(b) == COIN: w.remove(b)
  ```
- `overlap_box = (w: World, center: vec3, size: vec3, rot: vec4 = vec4(0, 0, 0, 1), mask: int = -1) -> Body[]`
- `overlaps = (w: World, c: Character) -> Body[]`

#### Rays

- `raycast = (w: World, a: vec3, b: vec3, mask: int = -1) -> Hit?`: the first body a ray from a to b meets (only those whose layer & mask is not 0; sensors are passed through)
- `sphere_cast = (w: World, a: vec3, b: vec3, r: f64, mask: int = -1) -> Hit?`: the first body a ball of radius r, moved from a to b, would touch: the hit's point is where it touches, and fraction how far along the ball's center is then (a thick ray: bullets that are not points, a camera kept out of walls, what is under a character's feet)

#### A character

- `struct Character`: A capsule that walks: it stands on what it can (slopes up to max_slope, steps up to step high), slides down steeper slopes, stops at walls, and keeps to the ground going down slopes and steps. It is not a body: the level and fixed and moving bodies stop it, and it shoves dynamic ones out of its way (as hard as its mass against theirs, times push), which stops it as much as they do not give way.
  ```gdscript
  struct Character:
      feet: vec3                      # the bottom of the capsule
      radius = 0.3
      height = 1.8
      velocity: vec3                  # as it moves (falling builds up here)
      on_ground = false
      ground_normal = vec3(0, 1, 0)
      standing_on = Body(0, 0)        # the body it stands on (a moving platform carries it)
      max_slope = 50deg
      step = 0.35
      layer = 1
      mask = -1
      mass = 80.0                     # (kilograms: what it shoves)
      push = 1.0
  ```
- `character = (w: World, feet: vec3, radius: f64 = 0.3, height: f64 = 1.8) -> Character`
- `walk = (w: mut World, c: mut Character, move: vec3, dt: f64, jump: f64 = 0)`: move c for dt seconds, at the velocity `move` along the ground (its y is not used), and jumping at `jump` meters a second when on the ground and jump > 0

## Networking

A module (`import net`): messages between programs over WebSockets, so a native build and a web page can play together (a server is a native program; a web page connects to one: [examples/netpong](https://github.com/david-andrew/sloppy-lang/tree/master/examples/netpong)), UDP datagrams between native programs, TCP and TLS streams, and `fetch` for what is at an http:// or https:// address.

```gdscript
# example: networking
import net

window("Chat", 960, 540)

# HOST=1: a server here; else a client of localhost (or ?join=address in a web page)
hosting = env("HOST") != none
if hosting: net.listen(4000)
else: net.connect(env("JOIN") ?? "localhost", 4000)
said: str[]

update = (dt: f64):
    loop e in net.receive():
        if e.kind == .joined: said.push("({e.peer} is here)")
        else if e.kind == .left: said.push("({e.peer} left)")
        else:
            said.push("{e.peer}: {e.text}")
            if hosting: net.send_all(e.data)       # (a server passes each message on)
    if key_pressed(.space): net.send_all("hello at {elapsed():.1} s")

draw = ():
    clear(rgb(0.08, 0.09, 0.12))
    text("Space: say hello", vec2(20, 20), 22)
    loop i in 0.. and line in said[max(said.len() - 18, 0)..]: text(line, vec2(20, 70 + 24 * i), 20)
```

Networking: messages between programs, over WebSockets, so that the same game reaches others from a native build and from a web page. `import net`, then:

```gdscript
net.listen(4000)                        # a server: others connect to this machine's port 4000
p = net.connect("192.168.1.20", 4000)   # a client: a peer (it connects in the background)
loop e in net.receive():                # what happened since the last call (every frame)
    if e.kind == .joined: ...           # a peer connected (to a server, or the server, to a client)
    if e.kind == .message: print(e.text)
    if e.kind == .left: ...             # it went away (or could not be reached)
net.send(p, "hello")                    # a str or u8[]; net.send_all(...) to every peer
```

Messages arrive whole and in order (WebSockets over TCP). A web page can only connect, not listen: the server is a native program (a game that hosts, or a small server of its own). A page served over https can only reach a wss:// server (TLS: put a proxy that has a certificate, such as Caddy, in front of the server); net.connect("wss://game.example.com") takes a URL as well, from a web page or a native program.

- `enum NetKind: joined, message, left`
- `struct NetEvent`
  ```gdscript
  struct NetEvent:
      kind: NetKind
      peer: int
      data: u8[]              # a message's bytes ...
      text = ""               # ... and as text
  ```

#### The peers

- `struct Handshake`: how a peer that connected to this program's server asked to (net.listen's, or an http server's that took a WebSocket): for a server that lets in only its own web pages (origin), or players who have signed in (a cookie, or a token in the query)
  ```gdscript
  struct Handshake:
      path = ""               # the address's path, as "/play" (its query apart)
      query: {str: str}       # ?room=4&name=ann: {"room": "4", "name": "ann"}
      headers: {str: str}     # names in small letters
      cookies: {str: str}
      origin = ""             # the site of the web page connecting ("https://example.com"; "" when not a page)
      address = ""            # where it connected from: "1.2.3.4"
  ```

#### Straight to another player, with no server (WebRTC)

- `invite = (reliable: bool = true) -> int`: One side invites: invite_code gives a short code to send the other player any way (a chat message). They join with it, and invite_code then gives them a reply code to send back, which the first side accepts. Then the peer is as any other: joined, messages, left. Web pages and native programs connect so, each to the other too; a STUN server (Google's, rtc.rtc_stun_servers) says how each looks from the internet, and nothing else is in between. reliable = false: messages may be lost or come in another order, never late (a fast game's state)
  ```gdscript
  p = net.invite()                     # inviting
  code = net.invite_code(p)            # "" for a moment, then the code to send
  net.accept_reply(p, their_reply)
  p = net.join_invite(their_code)      # joining: then net.invite_code(p) is the reply to send
  ```
- `join_invite = (code0: str) -> int`: join a game with the code its host sent, or a link with the code in it (…?invite=code): a peer, whose invite_code(p) is then the reply to send back
- `invite_code = (peer: int) -> str`: this side's code for a peer from invite or join_invite: "" while it is being made (a moment, as this machine's addresses are found)
- `webrtc_missing = () -> str`: why this game cannot connect straight to another player (invite, join_invite), in words for players: a web browser with WebRTC turned off (Firefox's media.peerconnection.enabled, a privacy setting); "" when it can
- `code_kind = (code: str) -> str`: what a code is: "invite" (from net.invite: for join_invite), "reply" (from join_invite: for accept_reply), or "" (not a code: cut short, or something else pasted); a link to an invite is one
- `accept_reply = (peer: int, reply: str) -> bool`: the other player's reply to this side's invite; false when it is not a reply code
- `turn_server = (url: str, user: str, password: str)`: a TURN server ("turn:host:port", or "host:port") with the user name and password it gives: the invites made after this also offer a path through it, a relay, for players whose networks allow none straight to each other (some mobile data, schools, offices). Your own (coturn), or a service's (some have a free tier); its password goes into every copy of the game. UDP only
- `listen = (port: int, largest: int = 16 << 20, host: str = "") -> bool`: start a server on port (native programs); false if the port cannot be had. largest: the biggest message taken from the peers that connect (one bigger ends its connection, with code 1009: a public server's guard), and a peer that has not finished connecting in 10 seconds is let go. host: the address it is reached at, "" every one this machine has ("127.0.0.1": from this machine only). handshake(peer) tells how each peer asked to connect (its path, cookies, the web page's site); an http server (import http) takes WebSockets on its own port too
- `stop_listening = ()`: stop taking new connections (the peers connected stay); listen starts again
- `connect = (host: str, port: int = 0) -> int`: connect to a server: a peer, connecting in the background (joined or left arrive by receive); host is a name or address and port its port, or host is a ws:// or wss:// URL (port then unused)
- `send = (peer: int, msg: u8[])`: send a message to a peer (nothing if it is not connected, or has gone): text goes as a text message (a string to a page's WebSocket), bytes as a binary one
- `send = (peer: int, msg: str)`
- `send_all = (msg: u8[])`: send a message to every connected peer
- `send_all = (msg: str)`
- `peers = () -> int[]`: the peers connected now
- `close = (peer: int)`: end a connection (a left event follows)
- `route = (peer: int) -> str`: how a peer is reached: "direct" (WebRTC, straight between the two), "relay" (WebRTC through a TURN server), "websocket" (a connection to a server, or one a server took), or "" (not known yet, or gone; a web page's browser does not tell)
- `backlog = (peer: int) -> int`: how much waits to reach a peer: over WebRTC, the pieces sent and not acknowledged yet and those waiting for room (in a web page, the bytes its data channel holds); over a WebSocket, the bytes not sent yet (0 in a web page)
- `dropped = (peer: int) -> int`: the messages to a peer dropped so far for want of room (one invited with reliable = false, whose messages may be lost: while the other side is slow to take them, newer ones are not held up)
- `why = (peer: int) -> str`: why a peer could not connect, once it has left ("": it did not say; a web page's browser does not tell)
- `local_address = () -> str`: this machine's address on its network (as 192.168.1.20): what another machine there connects to, and what a host tells the players joining it; "" when it has none (no network, or a web page)
- `receive = () -> NetEvent[]`: what happened since the last call: peers joining and leaving, and their messages, in order
- `receive = (only: int[]) -> NetEvent[]`: the same, for the given peers only (the others' events wait for another call): parts of a program that each have peers of their own, as a game hosting a relay and playing through it
- `receive = (only: int[], wait: f64) -> NetEvent[]`: the same, waiting up to `wait` seconds for the first of their events: a task's, for the peers it serves (a server's connection each, from accept). The peers waited for are its own from then on: receive() and receive_server() leave their events. In a web page, in a task only
  ```gdscript
  talk = (p: int):
      loop not net.gone(p):
          loop e in net.receive([p], wait = 30.0):
              if e.kind == .message: net.send(p, "you said {e.text}")
  ```
- `accept = (wait: f64 = 0.0) -> int`: a peer that connected to this program's server (listen's port, or an http server's websocket()), waiting up to `wait` seconds for one; -1 if none came. It is the caller's own, its joined event taken: its events come from receive([peer], ...), not receive() or receive_server(), so that a task can serve each one
  ```gdscript
  loop not stopping():
      p = net.accept(wait = 1.0)
      if p >= 0: spawn((): talk(p))
  ```
- `gone = (peer: int) -> bool`: the peer has gone (or never was): its left event has come, or is waiting to be taken
- `receive_server = () -> NetEvent[]`: the same, for the peers that connected to this program's server (listen) only
- `wait = (seconds: f64) -> bool`: wait until something comes for the program, or `seconds` pass: a message or a peer (receive), a connection's bytes or one to accept (the tcp_ functions), a fetch's answer (fetch_result), a request for its http server; true when something came (false: the time passed, or the program was asked to stop, as stopping() then says, or the task was cancelled). Meanwhile what waits to go out is sent as the connections take it. For a program with no window, which has nothing else to do: a game's frames come anyway
- `unsent = (peer: int) -> int`: how many bytes sent to a peer still wait to go out to it (a peer that reads slowly, or has stopped: one with more than 8 MB waiting is let go, so that it does not hold the server's memory)
- `handshake = (peer: int) -> Handshake`: how a peer asked to connect to this program's server (an empty Handshake for others)

#### UDP (native programs)

- `struct Datagram`
  ```gdscript
  struct Datagram:
      host: str               # where it came from: an address, "1.2.3.4"
      port: int
      data: u8[]
  ```
- `udp = (port: int = 0) -> int`: a UDP socket on port (0: one the system picks), or -1 (in use, or in a web page)
- `udp_send = (sock: int, host: str, port: int, data: u8[]) -> bool`: false if host is not known or it could not be sent (a datagram may also be lost on the way)
- `udp_send = (sock: int, host: str, port: int, text: str) -> bool`
- `udp_receive = (sock: int) -> Datagram[]`: the datagrams that have arrived since the last call
- `udp_port = (sock: int) -> int`: the port a UDP socket is on (as one made with udp(0), which the system picked), or -1
- `udp_close = (sock: int)`

#### TCP: a stream of bytes to one other program

- `tcp_connect = (host: str, port: int, timeout: f64 = 5.0) -> int`: a connection to host's port, waiting up to `timeout` seconds for it; -1 if it cannot be made (tcp_why says why)
- `tls_connect = (host: str, port: int = 443, timeout: f64 = 10.0) -> int`: a connection to host's port over TLS (443: https's), waiting up to `timeout` seconds for it; -1 if it cannot be made, or the server's certificate is not one this computer trusts for host (tcp_why says why). What it sends and receives goes through tcp_send, tcp_receive, tcp_close
- `tcp_why = () -> str`: why the last tcp_connect or tls_connect gave -1
- `trust_certificate = (pem: str) -> int`: a certificate authority to trust from now on, besides those the system trusts: its certificate as PEM text (what a .pem or .crt file holds, -----BEGIN CERTIFICATE----- ...), for servers whose certificates it signed (a test server's, a private network's); how many certificates were taken. The SLOPPY_CA_FILE environment variable names such a file for any program
- `tcp_listen = (port: int, host: str = "", backlog: int = 511) -> int`: a server on port: connections come from tcp_accept; -1 if the port cannot be had. host: the address it is reached at, "" every one this machine has ("127.0.0.1": from this machine only); backlog: how many connections may wait to be accepted
- `tcp_accept = (server: int, wait: f64 = 0.0) -> int`: a connection made to the server (waiting up to `wait` seconds for one), or -1 if none came (or the server was closed meanwhile)
- `tcp_address = (c: int) -> str`: the address a connection accepted by tcp_accept came from ("1.2.3.4"; "" for others)
- `tcp_send = (c: int, data: u8[], wait: f64 = 30.0) -> bool`: all of data sent, waiting while the other side is slow to take it (up to `wait` seconds while it takes none); false if the connection has failed or closed, or nothing went for that long
- `tcp_send = (c: int, text: str, wait: f64 = 30.0) -> bool`
- `tcp_write = (c: int, data: u8[]) -> bool`: data sent without waiting: what the other side has not taken yet goes later, as it takes it (in this connection's later tcp_ calls, and in net.wait); false if the connection has failed or closed. A server's way to send: one slow reader holds up nothing else (tcp_unsent says how much waits for it)
- `tcp_write = (c: int, text: str) -> bool`
- `tcp_unsent = (c: int) -> int`: how many bytes sent with tcp_write still wait for the other side to take them
- `tcp_receive = (c: int, wait: f64 = 0.0) -> u8[]`: the bytes that have arrived (waiting up to `wait` seconds for the first); empty when none has (or the connection has ended: tcp_closed says; or it was closed meanwhile, by another task)
- `tcp_closed = (c: int) -> bool`: the other side has closed the connection (or it failed), and everything sent has been received
- `tcp_close = (c: int, wait: f64 = 5.0)`: end a connection (or stop a server of tcp_listen's), after sending what tcp_write left waiting (for up to `wait` seconds). A task waiting in another tcp_ call on it is woken, and finds it closed

#### HTTP: what is at a web address

- `struct HttpReply`
  ```gdscript
  struct HttpReply:
      status: int             # the server's answer: 200 when all went well (404: no such page...);
                              # 0 when there was none (why says why; a redirect not followed, after
                              # five, is given as it came, with a why too)
      data: u8[]              # what it sent ...
      text = ""               # ... as text
      headers: {str: str} = {}    # its headers, their names in small letters (one sent more than
                                  # once: its values joined by ", ")
      why = ""
      size = 0                # how many bytes it sent (saved to a file: data and text are empty)
      all_headers: (str, str)[] = []  # every header as it came, in order (each Set-Cookie)
  ```
- `header_values = (r: HttpReply, name: str) -> str[]`: every value of a header the answer has (as each Set-Cookie), in order; name in any letters
- `fetch = (url: str, wait: f64 = 30.0, method: str = "GET", headers: {str: str} = {}, body: str = "", save_to: str = "", progress: (int, int) -> void = (got: int, all: int): pass) -> HttpReply`: a request to an http:// or https:// address, and its answer (following where it redirects, five times at most), waiting up to `wait` seconds in all; https is checked as a browser checks it (tls_connect). A GET unless method says otherwise; headers are sent besides the usual ones (as "Authorization": "Bearer ..."), and body after them (its Content-Type, unless given: JSON when it starts with { or [, else text). With save_to, a successful answer (2xx) goes to that file as it comes, not into data; progress(bytes so far, all of them or -1 when not said) is called as they come.
  ```gdscript
  r = net.fetch("https://api.example.com/items", method = "POST", body = to_json(item),
                headers = {"Authorization": "Bearer {token}"})
  net.fetch(url, save_to = "big.zip", progress = (got: int, all: int): print("{got} of {all}"))
  ```
- `fetch_start = (url: str, wait: f64 = 30.0, method: str = "GET", headers: {str: str} = {}, body: str = "", save_to: str = "", progress: (int, int) -> void = (got: int, all: int): pass) -> int`: the same request, started: an id for fetch_result, which gives the answer once it has come. Nothing waits for it meanwhile (in a web page, the browser asks; its server must allow the page's site, CORS)
- `fetch_result = (f: int) -> HttpReply?`: a fetch's answer (fetch_start's id), once it has come (status 0 when it failed: why says why); none while it is on its way. Given once: the fetch is done with after that, and asking again stops the program (a mistake: keep the answer when it comes)
- `fetch_cancel = (f: int)`: stop a fetch on its way (its answer is not given)
- `fetch_all = (urls: str[], at_once: int = 8, retries: int = 2, wait: f64 = 30.0, method: str = "GET", headers: {str: str} = {}, body: str = "", done: (int, HttpReply) -> void = (i: int, r: HttpReply): pass) -> HttpReply[]`: many requests, at most at_once of them on their way at a time (Go's worker pool around http.Get, an errgroup with SetLimit): their answers, in the order of urls. One that fails (status 0: no answer, or none in `wait` seconds) or whose server is busy or broken (429, 500, 502, 503, 504) is asked again, `retries` more times at most, after half a second, then 1, 2, 4... (or after the Retry-After the server asks for, a minute at most), unless it is a POST or a PATCH (which may have been done: those are not asked again); the other options are fetch's, for each one. done(i, r) is called as each answer comes (r is that of urls[i]): for a program that shows how far it is, or goes on with each answer at once. A program that asks stopping() and is asked to stop (ctrl-c) gets the answers that came, the rest with status 0. (A program with more to do as they come, a game's frames or a server's requests, uses fetch_start and fetch_result itself, as this does)
  ```gdscript
  rs = net.fetch_all(urls, at_once = 16)
  loop i in 0.. and r in rs:
      if r.status != 200: eprint("{urls[i]}: {r.status} {r.why}")
  ```

## HTTP servers

A module (`import http`): a web site, an API or a game's page served from a native program: routes with `:names`, JSON, cookies, files, server-sent events, and WebSockets on the same port (as net's peers). It waits with `net.wait`, so a slow client holds up no other; behind Caddy it serves https ([writing a server](START.md#writing-a-server)).

```gdscript
# example: HTTP servers
# a to-do list's API on http://localhost:8080 (curl localhost:8080/todos), until ctrl-c
import http

struct Todo:
    id = 0
    title = ""
    done = false

struct TodoIn:
    title = ""

todos: Todo[]

http.get("/todos", (r: http.Request) -> http.Response: http.json(todos))
http.get("/todos/:id", (r: http.Request) -> http.Response:
    id = to_int(r.params["id"]) ?? -1
    loop t in todos:
        if t.id == id: return http.json(t)
    http.json_error(404, "no todo {id}"))
http.post("/todos", (r: http.Request) -> http.Response:
    t = TodoIn()
    if let bad = http.read_json(r, t): return bad
    if len(trim(t.title)) == 0: return http.json_error(422, "a todo needs a title")
    todos.push(Todo(todos.len() + 1, trim(t.title)))
    http.json(todos.last(), 201))
http.files("/", "public")                   # the rest: the files in public/

http.log_requests = true
if not http.serve(8080): print("port 8080 is taken")
```

HTTP servers: a web site, an API, a game's page with its WebSockets on the same port. `import http`, then routes (a method and a path, with :names for parts that vary), each answered by a function from a Request to a Response:

```gdscript
http.get("/", (r: http.Request) -> http.Response: http.html("<h1>Hello</h1>"))
http.get("/todos/:id", (r: http.Request) -> http.Response:
    if let t = todos.get(to_int(r.params["id"]) ?? -1): return http.json(t)
    http.status(404))
http.post("/todos", add_todo)
http.files("/", "public")                   # the files under public/ (index.html for /)
http.serve(8080)                            # answers until ctrl-c (or SIGTERM)
```

A program that does more than answer (a chat whose WebSockets come through net, a job that fetches meanwhile) answers what has come each time round its own loop:

```gdscript
http.listen(8080)
loop not stopping():
    http.answer()
    loop e in net.receive(): ...
    net.wait(1.0)                           # until something comes (a request, a message)
```

Each request's handler runs as a task of its own (lib/std/task.jo): one that waits (a net.fetch, sleep, a channel) lets the others be answered meanwhile; one connection's requests are answered in order. Tasks take turns on the program's one thread, so handlers change the program's variables (a map of todos, a count) with no locks: nothing else runs between two waits. One that computes for long, runs a program (run_program) or reads a big file holds up the others while it does. A client slow to send or to read holds up no other (what waits to go out to it goes as it takes it). A handler that panics is answered 500 and the server goes on (the panic and its backtrace are written to standard error; what the handler had changed stays changed). Behind a proxy that has the certificate (Caddy: docs/START.md's "Writing a server"), the same program serves https. Native programs only.

Asked to stop (ctrl-c, SIGTERM), serve takes no more requests, gives the handlers running up to `drain` seconds to answer, and returns: the program then ends what it runs besides (closing the channels its tasks read), and saves. A program with its own loop calls shutdown() itself.

Trying a server from the same program (a test, Go's httptest): serve in a task, and fetch from it:

```gdscript
http.listen(18080, host = "127.0.0.1")
spawn((): http.serve(18080), background = true)
assert(net.fetch("http://127.0.0.1:18080/todos/1").status == 200)
```

- `struct Request`
  ```gdscript
  struct Request:
      method = ""             # "GET", "POST", ...
      path = ""               # "/todos/3": decoded (%20 a space), without the query
      query: {str: str}       # ?done=true&q=a%20b: {"done": "true", "q": "a b"}
      params: {str: str}      # the route's :names: /todos/:id gives {"id": "3"}
      headers: {str: str}     # names in small letters
      cookies: {str: str}
      body: u8[]              # what was sent with it ...
      text = ""               # ... as text (read_json reads it into a struct)
      target = ""             # the path and query as sent: "/todos?done=true"
      version = "HTTP/1.1"
      address = ""            # where it came from: "1.2.3.4" (behind a proxy, the proxy's; Caddy puts
                              # the client's in X-Forwarded-For)
      conn = 0                # its connection (a stream's: send_event and send_chunk go to it)
  ```
- `struct Response`
  ```gdscript
  struct Response:
      status = 200
      headers: {str: str}     # (Content-Length, Date and Connection are added)
      body: u8[]
      cookies: str[]          # each a Set-Cookie header (set_cookie makes them)
      file = ""               # a file sent as the body, a piece at a time (file() sets it)
      stream = false          # the body is sent later, by send_chunk or send_event (stream(), events())
      upgrade = false         # (websocket(): the connection becomes a net peer ...)
  ```

#### Routes

- `route = (method: str, pattern: str, handler: (Request) -> Response)`: a route for requests of that method ("*": any) to paths of the pattern
- `get = (pattern: str, handler: (Request) -> Response)`
- `post = (pattern: str, handler: (Request) -> Response)`
- `put = (pattern: str, handler: (Request) -> Response)`
- `patch = (pattern: str, handler: (Request) -> Response)`
- `delete = (pattern: str, handler: (Request) -> Response)`
- `files = (prefix: str, dir: str)`: the files under dir, at the paths under prefix ("/": the site's root): GET and HEAD, with each file's type, index.html for a directory (a directory asked for without its / is sent there), and what lets a browser keep a copy (ETag, Last-Modified: a file unchanged since is answered 304). Names starting with a dot (.git, .env) and paths with .. are not served

#### Answers

- `text = (s: str, status: int = 200, type: str = "text/plain; charset=utf-8") -> Response`: text (as text/plain, unless type says otherwise)
- `html = (s: str, status: int = 200) -> Response`: a web page's HTML
- `json = (value, status: int = 200) -> Response`: any value as JSON (to_json's): a struct, an array, a map...; JSON text already made goes with text(s, type = "application/json")
- `binary = (b: u8[], type: str = "application/octet-stream", status: int = 200) -> Response`: bytes, of a type ("image/png"...)
- `status = (code: int, message: str = "") -> Response`: a status with its reason as the text ("404 Not Found"), or with the text given
- `json_error = (code: int, message: str) -> Response`: an error for an API's client: {"error": message} as JSON
- `redirect = (to0: str, status: int = 302) -> Response`: to another address (302: for now; 301: for good; 303: after a form's POST, a GET of it)
- `file = (r: Request, path: str) -> Response`: a file, sent a piece at a time (a big one is never all in memory), with its type and what lets a browser keep a copy (as files() does); 404 when there is no such file
- `read_json[T] = (r: Request, into: mut T) -> Response?`: the request's body, as JSON, read into `into` (a struct, an array...: what from_json reads): none when it went well, else the answer to give (400, saying what is wrong with it), and `into` is as it was. The fields the body has are set, the others keep the values they had (a struct in it too, field by field; an array or a map in it is replaced), so the same reads a new one and changes one, a PATCH:
  ```gdscript
  t = TodoIn()
  if let bad = http.read_json(r, t): return bad
  ...
  t = todos[id]                                   # (PATCH /todos/:id with {"done": true})
  if let bad = http.read_json(r, t): return bad
  todos[id] = t                                   # (its title as it was)
  ```
- `set_cookie = (res: mut Response, name: str, value: str, max_age: int = -1, path: str = "/", http_only: bool = true, secure: bool = false, same_site: str = "Lax", domain: str = "")`: a Set-Cookie on the answer: the client sends the cookie back with its requests from then on (r.cookies), for max_age seconds (-1: until the browser closes). http_only: not for the page's JavaScript; secure: over https only; same_site: "Lax", "Strict" or "None"
- `clear_cookie = (res: mut Response, name: str, path: str = "/")`: a Set-Cookie that has the client forget a cookie

#### Streams: server-sent events and chunks

- `stream = (type: str = "text/plain; charset=utf-8") -> Response`: an answer whose body is sent later, a chunk at a time (send_chunk, then end_stream)
- `events = () -> Response`: a stream of server-sent events, what a web page's EventSource reads (send_event)
- `send_chunk = (conn: int, b: u8[]) -> bool`: a chunk of a stream's body; false when the client has gone (or has 8 MB waiting for it, a reader that stopped: then it is let go)
- `send_chunk = (conn: int, text: str) -> bool`
- `send_event = (conn: int, data: str, event: str = "") -> bool`: an event to an events() stream: its data (lines of text), and its name when not "message" (a page's EventSource: addEventListener(event, ...))
- `end_stream = (conn: int)`: the end of a stream: its body is complete (the connection may carry other requests)

#### WebSockets

- `websocket = (r: Request, largest: int = 16 << 20) -> Response`: the answer that makes the request's connection a WebSocket (a request that is not one's: 426). largest: the biggest message taken from it (a bigger one ends the connection, with code 1009)

#### Serving

- `serve = (port: int, host: str = "", largest: int = 1 << 20, backlog: int = 511, drain: f64 = 10.0) -> bool`: answer requests on port until the program is asked to stop (ctrl-c, SIGTERM: stopping()), then give the handlers running up to `drain` seconds to answer (shutdown); false at once when the port cannot be had. host, largest, backlog: as listen's
- `shutdown = (wait: f64 = 10.0) -> bool`: stop serving: no more requests are taken, the handlers running are waited for (up to `wait` seconds) and their answers sent, and the connections are closed (streams too). true when every one was answered in time (a handler still running then goes on, but its answer goes nowhere)
- `listen = (port: int, host: str = "", largest: int = 1 << 20, backlog: int = 511) -> bool`: start taking requests on port (answer() answers them); false when the port cannot be had (another program has it). host: the address it is reached at, "" every one this machine has ("127.0.0.1": from this machine only, as behind a proxy on it); largest: the biggest body taken (a bigger one is answered 413); backlog: how many connections may wait to be taken
- `stop_listening = ()`: stop taking requests (those of connections open are still answered)
- `answer = () -> int`: answer the requests that have come, and send what waits to go out; how many were answered. It does not wait: net.wait does (until there is something to answer)
- `log_line = (r: Request, res: Response, seconds: f64) -> str`: the line a request makes in a log: who, what, the answer's status and size, how long it took
  ```gdscript
  127.0.0.1 "GET /todos?done=true HTTP/1.1" 200 312 0.4ms
  ```
- `http_date = (t: f64) -> str`: a time (unix_time's seconds) as HTTP writes one: "Sun, 06 Nov 1994 08:49:37 GMT"
- `from_http_date = (s: str) -> f64?`: an HTTP date ("Sun, 06 Nov 1994 08:49:37 GMT") as unix_time's seconds; none when it is not one
- `reason = (code: int) -> str`: a status's reason: 404 "Not Found"
- `mime_type = (path: str) -> str`: the type of a file by its extension, as a Content-Type ("text/html; charset=utf-8" for .html); application/octet-stream for one not known

## Invite a friend

A module (`import invite`): the screens that connect two players with invite codes and no server: a link to send in a chat (it opens the game in a browser), a code sent back, copying, sharing and pasting (on phones too), and why when no connection was made. A game's menu calls `invite.start()` or `invite.join()`, and draw shows `invite.screen()` until it gives the other player. See [examples/duel](https://github.com/david-andrew/sloppy-lang/tree/master/examples/duel).

```gdscript
# example: invite
# (two players, anywhere: Invite a friend gives a link to send; once connected, each moves a dot
# with the arrows and sees the other's)
import net
import invite

friend = -1
mine = vec2(640, 360)
theirs = vec2(640, 360)
invite.from_link()

update = (dt: f64):
    global friend, theirs
    if friend < 0: return
    mine += input_axis() * f32(300.0 * dt)
    net.send(friend, "{mine.x} {mine.y}")
    loop e in net.receive():
        if e.kind == .message:
            w = split(e.text, " ")
            theirs = vec2(f32(to_float(w[0]) ?? 0.0), f32(to_float(w[1]) ?? 0.0))
        else if e.kind == .left: friend = -1

draw = ():
    global friend
    clear()
    if friend >= 0:
        circle(mine, 20, rgb(0.4, 0.8, 1.0))
        circle(theirs, 20, rgb(1.0, 0.6, 0.3))
        return
    if invite.active():
        if let c = invite.screen("the example"): friend = c.peer
        return
    ui_begin("menu", vec2(640, 300))
    if ui_button("Invite a friend"): invite.start(reliable = false)
    if ui_button("Join a friend"): invite.join()
    ui_end()
```

- `struct Invited`: the other player, once connected
  ```gdscript
  struct Invited:
      peer: int                   # (a net peer: net.send, net.receive; -1: this side hosts the room Play with anybody found, with no one yet)
      inviter: bool               # this side invited (and the other joined)
  ```
- `start = (reliable: bool = true, players: int = 2)`: show Invite a friend: an invite is made at once (reliable = false: lost messages are not sent again, nor do they hold up later ones; for fast games and rollback). With a matchmaking server, this computer's room opens instead, for players in all (this side too: 0 for any number, until close_room); the screen has Invite with a code instead
- `start_codes = (reliable: bool = true)`: show Invite a friend with codes (a link to send, a code sent back), with a matchmaking server too: for a game's own Invite with a code instead
- `anybody = (reliable: bool = true)`: Play with anybody (with a matchmaking server): the server finds a public room of this game that has room, or makes this side the host of a new one, which others who ask then join. The screen says how it goes; it gives the host Invited(-1, true) at once (the others then come by arrivals, as to a room), and the others the host (Invited(peer, false)). room_busy(true) sends those who come to another room
- `join = (code: str = "")`: show Join a friend, with their link or code if given (joined at once): an invite's, or a room's (with a matchmaking server: the short code, or a link with ?room= in it)
- `from_link = () -> bool`: the program was opened from an invite link (a web page's ?invite=... or ?room=..., or INVITE=code or ROOM=code): Join a friend with it, and true
- `active = () -> bool`: one of the screens is up
- `code = () -> str`: this side's code: the invite, or the reply to one ("" until it is made, a moment after start or join): for a game that shows it in its own way
- `reply = (code: str) -> bool`: the friend's reply to this side's invite, as Connect takes it from the box: false if it is not one
- `cancel = ()`: leave the screens (Back does it too), dropping the invite if it has not connected (and the room, if no friend has come through it)
- `screen = (game: str = "the game", web_page: str = "") -> Invited?`: draw the screen that is up (in draw), and follow the connection: none until the other player is connected, then them (the screens are gone then). game names the game in what it says; web_page is where it is on the web, for a computer's invite to link to

#### Rooms, through a matchmaking server (tools/rendezvous.jo)

- `server = (url: str)`: the matchmaking server for rooms (tools/rendezvous.jo: docs/MATCHMAKING.md runs one), as wss://match.example.com (web pages and native programs both reach it there; a page served over https may only reach wss://). "" for none. SLOPPY_SERVER (a web page's ?server=) sets it for any game, over the game's own ("none": no server): for trying a server of one's own
- `has_server = () -> bool`: there is a matchmaking server (rooms: start opens one, join takes a room's code)
- `room_code = () -> str`: this computer's room code while its room is open ("" when it is not): to show in a game's lobby
- `room_link = (web_page: str = "") -> str`: a link to the open room: this page's address (or web_page) with ?room= ("" with neither, or no room)
- `open_room = (reliable: bool = true, players: int = 0)`: open this computer's room, as start does, but with no screen: for a game that shows its code (room_code, room_link) and how it is going (room_status) in a lobby of its own. Friends come by arrivals(). Nothing without a matchmaking server (has_server); room_status says why when it cannot open
- `room_public = () -> bool`: the open room is a public one, which Play with anybody (anybody) found or made
- `room_status = () -> str`: what the room is doing, in words ("" when it is not open; why not, when open_room could not open it)
- `arrivals = () -> Invited[]`: friends who have connected through the open room since the last call (the first, while the screen is up, comes from screen): each a peer, as screen's. Call it every frame while hosting
- `room_busy = (on: bool)`: while on, friends who come to the room wait (told so) until it is off (to a public room: they are sent to another): for a game whose fight has started (call it every frame, with whether that is so)
- `close_room = ()`: close the room: friends can no longer join with its code (until start opens it again)
- `new_room_code = ()`: a new code for this computer's room (the old one, and links with it, no longer lead here)

## Netplay

A module (`import netplay`): players on different machines in one game. With rollback (`session`), only inputs travel, and a late one is made up for by playing the last ticks again; with a server (`server`, `client`), the server runs the game and clients predict their own moves and draw others a little in the past. Sloppy's math is its own (no system math library, no fused multiply-add), so a tick comes out the same on Linux, Windows, macOS and the web. See [examples/duel](https://github.com/david-andrew/sloppy-lang/tree/master/examples/duel) and [examples/arena](https://github.com/david-andrew/sloppy-lang/tree/master/examples/arena), and [playing over a network](MULTIPLAYER.md) for choosing between them.

```gdscript
# example: netplay
# (two machines in one program, over a simulated network with 150 ms latency: the top half is what
# player 1's machine shows, the bottom half player 2's; steer player 1 with the arrows)
import netplay

struct Pad:
    x = 0
struct Game:
    xs: f32[] = [400, 800]

tick = (g: Game, pads: Pad[]) -> Game:
    let g = g
    loop i in 0..2: g.xs[i] = clamp(g.xs[i] + 6 * pads[i].x, 20, 1260)
    g

a = netplay.session(Game(), tick, players = 2, me = 0)
b = netplay.session(Game(), tick, players = 2, me = 1)
link = netplay.Link(latency = 0.15)
now = 0.0

update = (dt: f64):
    now += 1.0 / 60.0
    a.play(Pad(int(round(input_axis().x))))
    b.play(Pad(int(sign(sin(now * 1.3)))))                 # (player 2 plays by itself)
    loop o in a.outgoing(): link.send(o.0, o.1, now)
    loop o in b.outgoing(): link.send(o.0, o.1, now)
    loop d in link.deliver(now):
        if d.0 == 0: a.receive(d.1)
        else: b.receive(d.1)

draw = ():
    clear(rgb(0.08, 0.09, 0.12))
    loop half in 0.. and g in [a.state, b.state]:
        y = 180 + 360 * half
        circle(vec2(g.xs[0], y), 24, rgb(0.35, 0.7, 1.0))
        circle(vec2(g.xs[1], y), 24, rgb(1.0, 0.6, 0.3))
    line(vec2(0, 360), vec2(1280, 360), 2, gray(0.3))
    text("rollbacks: {a.rollbacks} and {b.rollbacks}", vec2(20, 20), 22, gray(0.6))
```

- `struct Session[S, I]`
  ```gdscript
  struct Session[S, I]:
      step: (S, I[]) -> S
      players: int
      me: int
      delay = 2
      max_ahead = 10                  # (no more than __NP_HIST / 2)
      tick = 0                        # the tick state is at
      state: S
      waiting = false                 # play could not move on (too far ahead of someone's inputs)
      rollbacks = 0                   # how many times a tick was played again, so far
      longest_rollback = 0
      desync = -1                     # the first tick at which two machines' games differed (-1: none)
      problem = ""                    # what is wrong, said once on stderr too: packets that cannot be
                                      # read (another build?), two players with one number, a
                                      # player not heard from for 5 s, the games differing
  ```
- `session[S, I] = (start: S, step: (S, I[]) -> S, players: int, me: int, delay: int = 2) -> Session[S, I]`: a session of `players` players, this machine's being player `me` (from 0)
- `confirmed[S, I] = (s: Session[S, I]) -> int`: ticks below this have every player's input: they will not change again (some may not have been played here yet: those from s.tick on)
- `silent[S, I] = (s: Session[S, I], p: int) -> f64`: player p has not been heard from for this many seconds
- `stats_line[S, I] = (s: mut Session[S, I]) -> str`: one line on how play has gone since the last call, for a log (the game writes it where it likes, once a second, say): ticks played, frames spent waiting for someone's inputs, rollbacks and the longest, and for each other player the round trip, how long since heard from, and over net's peers how they are reached and what waits to go to them
- `ping[S, I] = (s: Session[S, I], p: int) -> f64`: the round trip to player p and back, in milliseconds (smoothed; 0 before it is known)
- `play[S, I] = (s: mut Session[S, I], input: I)`: this frame's input from this machine's player; the game moves on a tick (unless waiting)
- `outgoing[S, I] = (s: mut Session[S, I]) -> (int, u8[])[]`: what to send to whom since the last call: (player, bytes) (without s.udp)
- `receive[S, I] = (s: mut Session[S, I], data: u8[])`: bytes that arrived from another player (the packet says which)
- `stand_in[S, I] = (s: mut Session[S, I], p: int, input: I)`: this machine plays for player p from now on, with this input every tick (a game's "the computer flies it": the game's tick makes it so): for one who has left. Their inputs that reached this machine are kept, and the rest are this, so the others must have had none of theirs that this one did not: over net's peers, the host, who passes on all of them, can (or either of two players).
- `stands_in[S, I] = (s: Session[S, I], p: int) -> bool`: this machine plays for player p (s.stand_in)
- `lag[S, I] = (s: mut Session[S, I], ms: f64, jitter_ms: f64 = 0, loss: f64 = 0)`: simulate a slow network on what this session sends: ms more latency, up to jitter_ms more at random, and a share of packets lost (0 to 1)

#### Over net's peers (invite codes: web pages and native programs)

- `over[S, I] = (s: mut Session[S, I], peers: int[])`: play with the other players over peers from net: peers[p] is player p's (this machine's own entry is not used), from net.invite or net.join_invite (with no server: a page and a native program alike), or net.connect. The session's packets on them are its own (marked: the game's net.receive() leaves them); the game's own messages (a chat, "again?") and their joining and leaving are the game's, through net.send and net.receive as ever. For two, invited with net.invite(reliable = false) (packets lost are not resent: newer ones carry the same inputs, and nothing waits for an old one): For three or four, one hosts: it invites each of the others (each an invite and a reply), tells each its number (a message of the game's), and plays over all of them; the others play over the host alone (the rest -1), who passes their packets on: [-1, a, b] for the host, [host, -1, -1] for player 1.
  ```gdscript
  player 0: friend = net.invite(reliable = false) ... s.over([-1, friend])
  player 1: host = net.join_invite(code) ...          s.over([host, -1])
  ```

#### Over UDP (native programs)

- `udp[S, I] = (s: mut Session[S, I], port: int, addrs: (str, int)[])`: play over UDP: this machine listens on port, and each player p is at addrs[p] (this one's own entry is not used)

#### Through a relay (web pages too)

- `join_relay[S, I] = (s: mut Session[S, I], host: str, port: int, room: str, stun: str = "stun:stun.l.google.com:19302")`: play through the relay at host and port (or a ws:// or wss:// URL), with the players who name the same room
- `direct[S, I] = (s: Session[S, I], p: int) -> bool`: player p's packets come and go straight (WebRTC between web pages), not through the relay
- `relay = (port: int)`: a relay on port: call it every frame (in a game that hosts others), or relay_forever
- `relay_forever = (port: int)`: a program that is only a relay

#### A simulated network, for trying netplay in one program

- `struct Link`: packets between numbered ends, each arriving after latency (plus up to jitter at random), some lost; time is the caller's (seconds), so a test runs the same every time
  ```gdscript
  struct Link:
      latency = 0.05
      jitter = 0.0
      loss = 0.0
      seed: u64 = 1
  ```
- `send = (l: mut Link, to: int, data: u8[], now: f64)`
- `deliver = (l: mut Link, now: f64) -> (int, u8[])[]`: what has arrived by now: (to, data), in the order it arrives

#### Client and server

- `struct Server[W, I]`
  ```gdscript
  struct Server[W, I]:
      world: W
      apply: (W, int, I, W) -> W
      step: (W) -> W
      tick = 0
      send_every = 2                  # (30 worlds a second)
      joined: int[]                   # the players who came, and went, in the last update
      left: int[]
  ```
- `server[W, I] = (world: W, apply: (W, int, I, W) -> W, step: (W) -> W) -> Server[W, I]`
- `join[W, I] = (s: mut Server[W, I], player: int)`: a player connected (as the player number, any int); a player left
- `leave[W, I] = (s: mut Server[W, I], player: int)`
- `receive[W, I] = (s: mut Server[W, I], player: int, data: u8[])`: bytes from a player
- `hello[W, I] = (s: Server[W, I], p: int) -> str`: what player p's client gave connect (its name, a token to know it again by): "" until it has come, which is soon after p joined
- `waiting[W, I] = (s: Server[W, I], p: int) -> int`: how many of player p's inputs are waiting to be applied (1 or 2 normally; more: they come faster than the server ticks, which shows as lag)
- `lag[W, I] = (s: mut Server[W, I], ms: f64, jitter_ms: f64 = 0)`: simulate a slow network on what the server sends: ms more latency, up to jitter_ms more at random (kept in order, as over TCP); the client's lag does the same both ways
- `run[W, I] = (s: mut Server[W, I], ticks_per_second: f64 = 60.0)`: run the server: update 60 times a second, on time (each tick's start set from the first, so a slow sleep does not make it fall behind), until the program ends
- `outgoing[W, I] = (s: mut Server[W, I]) -> (int, u8[])[]`: what to send to whom since the last call (without listen)
- `listen[W, I] = (s: mut Server[W, I], port: int)`: serve over WebSockets on port: native and web clients connect (players are net's peers)
- `update[W, I] = (s: mut Server[W, I])`: one tick: each player's next input applied, the rest of the tick, and the world sent out
- `world_at[W, I] = (s: Server[W, I], tick: int) -> W`: the world as it was at an earlier tick (the latest kept, if that one is gone)
- `struct Client[W, I]`
  ```gdscript
  struct Client[W, I]:
      apply: (W, int, I, W) -> W
      state: W                        # the latest world from the server, with this player's newer inputs applied
      server: W                       # the latest world from the server, as it sent it
      me = -1                         # this player's number (once the server has said)
      server_tick = -1                # the tick of server
      interpolation = 6               # how far in the past others are drawn (view), in ticks
      ping = 0.0                      # milliseconds from an input sent to the world that answers it (smoothed)
      problem = ""                    # what is wrong, said once on stderr too: could not connect, the
                                      # server gone or not heard from for 5 s, worlds that cannot be read
  ```
- `client[W, I] = (start: W, apply: (W, int, I, W) -> W) -> Client[W, I]`
- `connect[W, I] = (c: mut Client[W, I], host: str, port: int, hello: str = "")`: connect to the server; hello: text the server gets as srv.hello(player) (a name, a token to know a player again by after a reconnect). A client that lost its connection, or called disconnect, connects again the same way
- `disconnect[W, I] = (c: mut Client[W, I])`: close the connection (connect again later, to the same server or another)
- `lag[W, I] = (c: mut Client[W, I], ms: f64, jitter_ms: f64 = 0)`: simulate a slow network on what the client sends and gets: ms more latency each way, up to jitter_ms more at random (kept in order, as over TCP)
- `connected[W, I] = (c: Client[W, I]) -> bool`: connected, and the server has sent a world (and is still heard from)
- `play[W, I] = (c: mut Client[W, I], input: I)`: this tick's input: applied here at once (prediction) and sent
- `receive[W, I] = (c: mut Client[W, I], data: u8[])`: bytes from the server
- `outgoing[W, I] = (c: mut Client[W, I]) -> u8[][]`: what to send since the last call (without connect)
- `view_tick[W, I] = (c: Client[W, I]) -> f64`: the server's tick being drawn for the others: some ticks in the past (fractional)
- `view[W, I] = (c: Client[W, I]) -> (W, W, f32)`: the two worlds around view_tick and how far between them (0 to 1): draw the others at mix(a's, b's, f)
